At call time the callers hand what they already read under `sc(u)` to `route()`, which validates it again (a pick the catalog later dropped falls back): web chat reads the conversation record (`getConversation(sc(u), conv)`, nothing read for the default thread or a malformed id) and routes `chatRouteFor(u, conversation)` once, before `reserveChatTurn`, so the count, the hold and the model called are that one model (`GET /api/chat` and the chat page show its hold); `generateBatch` routes `generateRouteFor(user, brand, type)` once after the brand read, so `ctx.task` is the batch's type. `confirmedChina` is always `u.chinaHostingOk === true` of the session's account (a member's session carries the owner's record), never a request field. A rejected pick runs the automatic choice and the customer is told, in the reply language (the request's `x-lang`, else the account's): chat puts `MODEL_UNAVAILABLE.chat` at the top of the reply, saved with it (so a reload shows it; a paused or mock reply made no model call and gets none), `POST /api/generate` answers `notice: MODEL_UNAVAILABLE.generate`, and the chat tools and MCP `generate_content` add `MODEL_UNAVAILABLE_NOTE` to their result for the assistant to relay. MCP `ask_noor` has no conversation record and routes `route("chat")`. The reviewer, the cross-review, the judge, onboarding, image prompts and the free tools never read a pick (`tests/model-override.test.ts`).
    7. The picker (2026-10-08, plan Task 7; `src/components/app/ModelPicker.tsx`, `src/lib/model-picker.ts`, copy in `src/lib/app-i18n.ts` `models`). Two places: the chat header (`ChatModelPicker`, one conversation's pick) and, under the brand form once the brand file exists, "Default model for content" (`BrandModelField`, the active brand's pick, with its own Save; `PUT /api/brand` never changes it). Each reads `GET /api/models` on mount and shows nothing of its own: the label, the tier, a "Hosted in China" badge for a `hosting: "china"` row, and the server's multiplier for that surface formatted `x0.6` / `x1` / `x2` (two decimals at most, trailing zeros trimmed); chat adds "Each message counts as N messages" when `chatUnits` is above 1 (Arabic in the dual, plural and singular forms), the brand page shows the range of the server's per-type `generateByTask` over the types the model may run (rule 902, e.g. `x1–2`) and words it "{x} per batch" / «{x} لكل دفعة» (a multiplier of the batch's credits, never a credit count). A row the server sent no finite multiplier for is left out, and the chat list also leaves out a row whose `tools` is false (the server refuses it there). Automatic is always first, has no multiplier, and sends `model: null`; the selected entry is the stored value while the list offers it, else Automatic. For chat the stored value is, latest first (`storedPick`): a pick made in this panel, the open conversation's record as the chat page read it (`getConversation(sc(u), conv)`, the same read that sets the allowance line's ceiling, passed as `active` with its id and `model` only, so a conversation older than the newest hundred listed still shows its pick), then `Conversation.model` from `GET /api/conversations`; for the brand `Brand.model` from the page's `getBrand`. A change is decided against the stored value itself (`pickChanges`): over a stored id the list no longer offers, Automatic shows as selected and choosing it (chat) or saving it (brand, Save enabled) sends `null`, which clears the stale record. The default thread has no record, so its picker explains "Start a new conversation to choose its model" instead of listing. Picking a China-hosted row while `chinaConfirmed` is false opens the app's confirmation dialog (`useConfirm`) once; only the owner, not a founder impersonating, is offered it (`canConfirmChina`, from the page: `can(u, "profile.account") && u.actor?.kind !== "founder"`), anyone else is told only the owner can allow it. On yes the picker sends `{confirmChina: true}` alone, then the pick; a refused confirmation never sends the pick. The change shows at once and rolls back on any refusal, as one pure step (`nextMadePicks` in the chat panel, keyed by the conversation the pick was made on, so a refusal that arrives after the person moved to another conversation restores that one; `nextBrandPick` for the brand field, which keeps the refused choice selected for another try unless the list dropped it). A chat pick that landed hands the answer's `chat` (`pickedChat`, shape-checked) to the panel, which replaces its allowance line with it as it came while that conversation is still the open one, so the overflow line's "at most N credits" is the new model's; the client works out nothing. The chat trigger is disabled while a message of the panel is in flight (`pickerLocked`, the panel passes `replying={busy}`): that reply's own status arrives after the pick's and would put the old model's ceiling back. Refusals: 503 or 409 `busy` says try again in a moment; `unknown_model`, `not_pickable` and `china_unconfirmed` say the model is not available and reload the list; `not_found` reloads the conversation list (chat) or asks to save the brand file first (brand); 401 goes to the login page; 403 on the confirmation says only the owner can allow it; 429 says too many changes this hour. The chat refusal note goes by itself after `NOTE_MS` (8 s), on Escape and on the next pick. The list is a plain list of buttons, the chosen one `aria-current`, reached with Tab; the trigger's `aria-controls` names the popover (which holds the list, the default-thread hint or the load failure) only while it is open, and Escape closes it and gives focus back to the trigger. The picker module imports only types from `models/tiers.ts` and the component nothing from `src/lib/models/` (`tests/client-bundle.test.ts`), and no picker string names a model (`tests/model-picker-copy.test.ts`).
    8. Metering by model and provider (2026-10-09, plan Task 8; `src/lib/usage.ts`, `src/lib/model.ts`, `src/lib/admin-stats.ts`, the Billing tab of `AdminConsole.tsx`). Global counters only, in the `_all` month, never per account: `meter` adds `usage:_all:{YYYY-MM}:model:{modelKey}:calls|usd_micro` (the catalog id, else `other`) and `usage:_all:{YYYY-MM}:provider:{anthropic|straitly}:calls|usd_micro` (the call's `provider` argument, else `effectiveProvider()`, `src/lib/model.ts`: `straitly` only with `LLM_PROVIDER=straitly` and `STRAITLY_API_KEY`, the same decision `anthropic()` makes, else `anthropic`; anything else is ignored) inside its existing `Promise.all`; the micro-dollars are the same number the other counters get, so `costUsd` and the rates are untouched. `withDirectFallback` adds one to `provider:fallback` each time it re-sends a request to Anthropic (fire and forget; a store error changes nothing about the retry or the logs). A pick `route()` refused is counted by `noteRejectedPick(purpose, reason)` under `pickreject:{chat|generate}:{reason}` where the caller reads `routed.rejected` (`POST /api/chat`, `generateBatch`), best effort: it never changes the turn or the batch and logs one fixed line on a store error. The month's numbers come back from `getUsage("_all")` (one `mget`; empty for an account) through `overview().modelUsage`. Counted from this release; no backfill.

## 9. Approval and rejection of a task

Files: `src/app/api/tasks/[id]/route.ts`, `src/lib/task-version.ts`, `src/components/app/Inbox.tsx:181`, `ChatPanel.tsx:43`, `TodoPanel.tsx` (walk Approve), `src/lib/secretary-walk.ts` (the walk card's `version`).

1. `PATCH /api/tasks/{id} {status?, editedBody?, note?, scheduledFor?, version?}`; session required; the change is applied with `updateTask(sc(u), id, ...)` (`tasks/[id]/route.ts:39`, step 9), so only tasks under the caller's active scope resolve (404 otherwise, `:80`). Since 2026-09-29 the role depends on the result: a request that leaves the item `approved` needs admin+ (`publish`) when the item was not approved before or its text changes; editing, rejecting, reopening and rescheduling need any login (`drafts`). The check runs before anything is saved: `drafts` before the task is read, `publish` inside the `updateTask` change on the task as stored, so an approval that landed while the request waited is judged correctly, and a refusal writes nothing and answers 403 `forbidden`. Preference notes, `approve`/`reject` tracking and the decision records (step 8) are collected inside the change and run after the response (`after()`). A request that needs `publish`, or sends `status: "approved"`, must carry `version`, the `taskVersion` (`src/lib/task-version.ts`: SHA-256 of title, `editedBody ?? body` and the generated image) of the card it was made from (rules 560-564). It is compared inside the `updateTask` change with the item as stored: missing or not a string is 400 `version_required`, different is 409 `{error: "changed", task}`, and nothing is saved (no status, no `decidedAt`, no decision, no preference note). The inbox, the chat card and Reem's walk card send it; on `changed` they show the item as it is now.
2. `scheduledFor`: `null` clears, `YYYY-MM-DD` sets.
3. `editedBody` different from `body`: stored (max 20000 chars). When `gov.learning` is on, a preference note is pushed to `prefs:{scope}` and appended to `preferences.md`. The reason typed with an edit or a rejection is `cleanNote(note)`: a string only, one line, control and direction characters removed, at most 300 characters (rules 565-566); the append to `preferences.md` runs under `prefsfile:{scope}` (`lockOwned`, rule 567).
4. `status` in `approved|rejected|pending`: sets `decidedAt` on change, tracks `approve`/`reject`, and on reject with a note records a preference.
5. Any status may be moved to any other status, including after publishing, within the role rule of step 1: approving an item, or rewriting one that stays approved, is the owner's or an admin's; everything else is any login's. The task itself does not say which login decided; each decision record does (`by`, step 8): the owner, a member with its own login and role, or the founder through support access (`byOf`, section 30).
6. Brand gate (`src/lib/brand-rules.ts`): when the resulting status is `approved` and the item is customer-facing (`CUSTOMER_FACING`: social, article, whatsapp, ads, gbp, followup_seq, cart_recovery), the final text (`title` plus `editedBody ?? body`) is checked against the brand file's banned words. A hit returns 409 `{error: "brand_violation", violations}` and nothing is saved. Saving an edit as `pending` is allowed. Matching folds Arabic diacritics, tatweel and letter variants, accepts the Arabic prefixes و ف ب ل ك ال and suffixes for Arabic terms of three letters or more, and requires whole words for Latin and shorter terms. The inbox runs the same function in the browser to label cards and to check the editor as the owner types.
7. Auto-approval goes through `initialStatus(autoApprove, brand, item)` at every creation site (`api/generate`, `api/mcp` `generate_content`, chat `generate_content` and `draft_items`, the weekly schedule): an item that breaks a rule is created `pending` even when its type is auto-approved, and the chat tool reports how many were held.
8. Decision records (2026-09-29, `src/lib/decisions.ts`). The route works out the request's human decisions inside its `updateTask` change, from the task as stored (step 9), and after the task is saved appends them to `decisions:{scope}` (scope from `sc(u)`, never from the body): `edit` when the stored text changes (re-saving the same edit adds nothing; `editPct` uses `editPctOf`, the light index's measure), then `approve`, `reject` or `reopen` (back to `pending`) when the status changes. The owner's typed `note` goes on the rejection, or on the edit when nothing was rejected, cut to 300 characters. Each record carries `by`, who decided (`byOf(u)`, `src/lib/actor.ts`, worked out from the session in the route's first `Promise.all`, before the response and before `after()`, never from the body; section 30 step 1), the owning agent from `agentForTask(type)`, the title (80) and the judge's score and automatic-fail code at that moment. Records are written whatever `gov.learning` says, and read back into the owning agent's prompts only while it is on (section 7 step 13); a request stopped by the brand gate records nothing; items created approved by `initialStatus` never appear. Tracking, decision records and preference notes go out in one `Promise.all` inside `after()`; `preferences.md` is then read and rewritten as before. `recordDecisions` also writes one `decision` activity row per record in the same round (section 32; not for `publish`).
9. Whole-task writes (`updateTask`, `src/lib/brand.ts:357`): `saveTask` replaces the whole task, so a writer that saves a copy it read before slow work erases whatever landed meanwhile, a render that finished by poll, webhook or sweep included. Every writer of an existing task goes through `updateTask` instead: the approve and edit route (`tasks/[id]/route.ts:39`), the publish route and the chat tool `publish_item`, both through `publishOnce` (`src/lib/publish-once.ts:108`, and `:91` for an `earlier_live` answer; called at `publish/[id]/route.ts:40` and `chat.ts:729`), the judge's scores (`judge.ts:145`), the placeholder card (`src/lib/media/start.ts:63`) and every task write of the media engine (the start's pointer `jobs.ts:190`, the start's cleanup `:247,257`, finalize `:470`, fail `:555`). Each one:
   1. takes `tasklock:{scope}:{id}` (SET NX, 10 s TTL), trying 60 times 50 ms apart, else throws `task_lock_timeout`;
   2. re-reads the task; a task deleted meanwhile returns null and is not recreated;
   3. runs the writer's `mutate` once on a copy of the stored task, which applies only that writer's fields (or returns null to save nothing, as the brand gate does on a 409, the placeholder does over artwork, and the engine does when the task no longer points at its job or, at finalize, already took it in `mediaApplied`);
   4. saves through `saveTask` (pending set and light index follow) and releases the lock; a release that fails is logged, not thrown, and the TTL frees the lock.

   Creation sites (`/api/generate`, MCP `generate_content`, the chat tools, the weekly schedule and the routines, all through `generateBatch`, section 8 step 13; MCP `save_draft`) still call `saveTask(..., true)` for new tasks; the decision records (step 8) are an append-only list, not a task write. The scope is always the caller's `sc(u)` or the one stored on the media job. So a clip that finishes during a publish, an approval, an edit or a judge run keeps its media and credits, and an approval landing during a finalize keeps its status.

Owner rewrite (2026-09-29): an admin or owner who saves new text on an item that stays approved and has no `decidedAt` (auto-approval had passed it) sets `decidedAt` in the same change (`tasks/[id]/route.ts`), with the edit's decision record, so the judge's veto (section 28) and the chat's "nobody reviewed it" (section 10 step 10) no longer apply to it.

## 10. Publishing to a connector

Files: `src/app/api/publish/[id]/route.ts`, `src/lib/task-version.ts`, `src/lib/connectors/publish.ts`, provider modules, chat tool `publish_item` (`chat.ts:708`).

1. `POST /api/publish/{id} {provider, version, again?, live?}`; session and admin+ (`publish`); task from `sc(u)`. `version` is required (400 `version_required`); it is compared with the item read by the route (409 `changed` with the task) and again in `publishOnce`'s `check` under the publish lock (step 9). The chat tool `publish_item` needs the same action but sends no version: it publishes only items a person approved (`decidedAt`), and every such approval was bound to a version (rule 561); new media on an approved, unpublished item clears `decidedAt`, so chat refuses it until a person approves again (rule 576).
2. `publishTask(scope, task, provider)`: loads and decrypts `conn:{scope}:{provider}`; throws `not_connected` when absent. WordPress and Shopify create drafts; Telegram and X publish live (`publish.ts:24-44`). WordPress (rules 618-622): a post is a draft unless the body says `live: true` for that one item (any other value is a draft; `live` on another platform is 400 `live_unsupported`; the site user must hold `publish_posts`, else 502 `wordpress_cannot_publish_live` before anything is sent, which leaves no result-unknown marker). The item's artwork (`hasArtwork`) is copied into `/wp-json/wp/v2/media` (https only, png/jpeg/webp/gif, at most 8 MB, through `safeFetch`) and set as `featured_media`; a failed copy still makes the draft and answers `notes: ["image_failed"]`. `again` updates the post named by the `id` in the item's own WordPress record (digits only; never an id from the request) and sends a status only when `live` is also set, so what the owner did in WordPress stays; the update replaces the title and body with Thakeen's text and does not touch the image; a 404 makes a new draft. The record stores `id` for WordPress only. The chat and MCP tool never pass `again` or `live`. Body is `editedBody ?? body`; X clips to 280 chars, Telegram to 4000.
2b. Salla has no article target (since 2026-10-05, rule 628). Salla's Merchant API has no endpoint to create a blog article or a draft, so the registry lists Salla with `products` and `orders` only, `targetsFor` never returns it, and the publish route answers `wrong_target` for it. When Salla is connected, an article card in the inbox shows a note (`copyOnlyNote` in `targets.ts`, text `sallaArticle`) that nothing is sent and the owner copies the text with the card's copy button into Salla's blog editor.
3. Before publishing, the route and the chat tool apply the brand gate from section 9 to the item's current text with `publish: true` (since 2026-09-29 no placeholder of any kind, not even a WhatsApp `{{1}}`, goes out), including items approved before the word was banned: 409 `brand_violation` from the route, a refusal naming the word from the tool. On success `publishOnce` (step 9), for the route and the chat tool alike, appends to `task.published` and force `status = "approved"` (the route also sets `decidedAt` when the item was pending: publishing it is the owner's decision), applied through `updateTask` (section 9 step 9) to the task as stored after the publish, not to the copy read before it, so a render that finished during the publish keeps its media and credits. The route answers with that stored task. Both the HTTP route and the chat tool refuse a provider `targetsFor` does not list (`wrong_target`); the route refuses a rejected item (the owner's click is the approval), and the chat tool publishes only items already approved.
4. Provider error messages (up to 200 chars of the remote response) are returned to the caller in `error` (`publish/[id]/route.ts:65`).
5. Nothing publishes on a schedule. `scheduledFor` only drives calendar display and "due" notifications (`notifications.ts:138`).
6. Targets (`connectors/targets.ts`, no runtime imports, shared by the route, the chat tool and the inbox buttons): `connectedTargets` turns the stored connections into provider ids and adds `facebook` when the Meta connection has a chosen Page (`meta.pageId`) and `instagram` when that Page has an Instagram professional account (`meta.igId`). `targetsFor` offers `facebook` for `social` and `cart_recovery` items on the `instagram` or `facebook` channel, and `instagram` only for those items on the `instagram` channel that carry a generated image (`hasArtwork` from `src/lib/media-catalog.ts` at `targets.ts:33`: an `imageUrl` with `imageProvider` fal, kie or higgsfield; a placeholder card, an image with no recorded provider and a clip are not offered). Both publish through the `meta` connection (`CONNECTION_OF`). A video is never a publish target in this round; the inbox says so under every clip (`videoNoPublish`).
7. Facebook (`connectors/meta.ts` `postToFacebook`): `POST /{page}/feed` with `message`, or `POST /{page}/photos` with `url` and `caption` when `hasArtwork(task)` (`publish.ts:46`), with the Page token as a Bearer header. `publishTask` checks `hasArtwork` again before an Instagram post (`publish.ts:51`, `instagram_needs_image`), so the rule holds at the call that posts, not only in the target list. Instagram (`postToInstagram`): `POST /{ig}/media` with `image_url` and `caption` (2,200 characters at most), `GET /{container}?fields=status_code` until `FINISHED` (8 tries, 1.5 s apart), `POST /{ig}/media_publish`, then the `permalink`. Markdown headings and bold are stripped from the text. Graph error code 190 is reported as 401 and codes 10 and 200 as 403, so `refusedToken` marks the Meta connection for reconnecting.
8. Publishing a `pending` item through the route is the owner's approval: it sets `decidedAt` and appends a `publish` record to `decisions:{scope}` (section 9 step 8; `publishOnce` sets `decidedAt` inside its `updateTask` change and reports `approving`, and the route records the decision). An item that was already approved, by the owner or by auto-approve, records nothing; a failed record is logged and does not turn a finished publish into an error. Every publish that went out, from the route or the chat tool `publish_item`, is one `published` activity row with the provider (section 32), in the same round as the tracking and the decision record; a refused, busy, duplicate or unknown attempt (step 9) returns before it and writes none. An `earlier_live` answer (step 9) is a post that went out: the attempt it answers ended `publish_unknown` and wrote nothing, so the route writes that attempt's row, and for a pending item its `publish` decision (`publishOnce` sets `decidedAt` to the attempt's time and reports `approving`), at the attempt's time before it answers 409. Only an `again` gets that answer, and the chat tool never sends one.
9. One publish per item and platform (2026-09-29, `src/lib/publish-once.ts`). The route and the chat tool send through `publishOnce(scope, id, provider, {again, check})`: it takes `publock:{scope}:{id}:{provider}` (70 s from the publish route, just above its 60 s; 330 s from chat and MCP, above their 300 s; a second press gets 409 `publish_busy`), reads the item and runs the caller's `check` on that copy (rejected, brand gate with `publish: true`, for the route the version, and for the chat tool the person's approval), refuses a platform with an attempt marker left behind (409 `publish_unknown`, checked first because it is newer) and a platform already in `task.published` (409 `already_published` with its `url`), unless `again` is true. It writes `pubtry:{scope}:{id}:{provider}` before calling the platform and removes it after the post is recorded. An error removes it only when it proves nothing went out (`mayHavePosted` reads only the connector's leading status, `x 403: …` or `meta feed 400: …`: a 4xx, a refused token, a duplicate and the checks made before sending are refusals); anything else, and a kill at the time limit, leaves it, and the answer is `publish_unknown` from the first attempt, so the owner checks the platform before pressing again. X and Telegram sends time out at 45 s, so a slow platform is answered before the route's 60 s kill. Telegram errors carry the HTTP status (`telegram 400: …`); X's 403 for a repeated text is `x_duplicate` and Facebook's `(#506)` is a duplicate too (409 `duplicate_post`), which no longer marks the X connection for reconnection. A duplicate answer to an `again` after an unknown attempt proves that attempt went live: it is recorded on the item (no URL, the attempt's time; a pending item becomes approved with `decidedAt` at that time), the route writes its records (step 8), and the answer is 409 `earlier_live` with that `at`. In the inbox a 5xx without a reason or no answer at all is shown as the "check the platform first" question, and the reason a publish button is off is written under the buttons, not only on hover. The inbox shows that question with a "Publish again" button (`Inbox.tsx`, `AGAIN`); the chat tool never repeats a publish.
10. The chat tool `publish_item` refuses an item only auto-approval passed (no `decidedAt` and never published by the owner) and refuses every call over MCP (2026-09-29). The inbox's publish buttons follow the publish-strength gate, so a WhatsApp draft with `{{1}}` (approved as a template) offers no Telegram post.

## 11. Connector OAuth: connect and callback

Files: `src/components/app/IntegrationsPanel.tsx:91,218`, `src/app/api/connect/[provider]/start/route.ts`, `.../callback/route.ts`, `src/lib/connectors/{registry,oauth,store}.ts`.

1. Browser navigates to `GET /api/connect/{provider}/start?lang=&shop=`. Session and admin+ (`connectors`) required. Provider must be `kind:"oauth"` and have both client env vars set (503 otherwise).
2. `shop` (Shopify only in practice): lowercased, scheme and path stripped, `.myshopify.com` appended when missing; anything that is then not `SHOP_HOST` (a single-label `*.myshopify.com`) is refused with 400 `bad_shop` before any redirect.
3. State: 16 random bytes hex. `oauthstate:{state}` = `{email: sc(u), provider, lang, shop, verifier, actor}` for 10 min, where `actor` is the acting login (2026-09-29). PKCE pair only when `oauth.pkce` (X). The same state goes into the `nashr_oauth` cookie (httpOnly, SameSite=Lax, Secure as the session cookie, path `/api/connect`, 10 min).
4. Redirect to the provider authorize URL with `redirect_uri = ${APP_ORIGIN}/api/connect/{provider}/callback`.
5. `GET .../callback?state=&code=`: the `nashr_oauth` cookie is read and cleared, and `takeState` reads and deletes the state (single use). Missing state or code: `error=`. A state issued for another provider: `error=invalid_state`. The cookie must equal the state (`sameSecret`) and the signed-in account must own the state's scope, else `error=session_mismatch` and no code exchange. The acting login must also be the state's `actor` (a state written before `actor` existed completes only for the owner), else `error=session_mismatch`; then `connectors` is checked again, so a role lost while the provider's page was open ends with `error=forbidden`. Shopify also needs a valid `hmac` over the query (`validShopifyHmac`, else `bad_hmac`) and a query `shop` equal to the state's (`bad_shop`).
6. `exchangeCode` posts to the provider token URL (Basic auth for X, body credentials otherwise; Shopify URL built from `shop`). All scalar fields of the token response are kept as `data`.
7. `saveConnection(st.email, ...)` writes `conn:{scope}:{provider}` (encrypted) and adds to `conns:{scope}`; the scope comes from the state record, not from the current cookie. `track(scope.split("#")[0], "connect")`.
8. Every use of a stored connection (publishing, product import) goes through `getFreshConnection` (`src/lib/connectors/fresh.ts`): an OAuth token that expires within 5 minutes is refreshed first (`grant_type=refresh_token`) under the lock `connlock:{scope}:{provider}`, and a rotated refresh token is saved. A refused refresh, a missing refresh token, or a 401/403 from the platform sets `needsReconnect` and the call fails with `reconnect_required`; the Integrations page then shows the connection with a Reconnect button.
9. A new connection saved by the callback replaces the record, which clears `needsReconnect`.
10. `DELETE /api/connect/{provider}` writes `null` to the connection key (the id remains in `conns:{scope}`; `listConnections` skips nulls). `POST /api/connect/{provider} {action:"import_products"}` imports products for shopify, woocommerce, odoo, prestashop, salla, zid into `products.md`. The Zid callback reads the manager profile (`GET /managers/account/profile`) and keeps the store id in `meta.storeId`, the store name in `meta.account` and its address in `meta.site`; every Zid call sends the `authorization` token as the bearer, `access_token` as `X-Manager-Token`, and `Store-Id`. A 403 on the Zid product import answers `zid_access_denied` without marking the connection for reconnecting (the Integrations card shows a permissions message); a 401 marks it. Roles: `DELETE`, `select_page`, `select_org` and `select_ad_account` need `connectors` and `import_products` needs `catalog.import` (all admin+); `sync_sales` and `sync_ads` are any login's (`read`); another action is 400 `bad_action`.
10. Meta (`connectors/meta.ts` `connectMeta`, run by the callback after the code exchange): the short-lived user token is traded for a long-lived one (`grant_type=fb_exchange_token`, app credentials in the POST body), `GET /me/accounts` lists the Pages with their Page tokens and linked Instagram accounts, and the first Page with Instagram (else the first Page) is chosen. `data` (encrypted) holds the user token, every Page token and the chosen `page_token`; `meta` holds only `pageId`, `igId`, `account` and, with more than one Page, `pages` (ids, names, Instagram usernames). No expiry is stored: Page tokens from a long-lived user token do not expire. An account with no Page returns `error=meta_no_page` and saves nothing. `POST /api/connect/meta {action:"select_page", pageId}` switches the Page only to one Meta returned (400 `unknown_page` otherwise). With `META_CONFIG_ID` set, the authorize URL sends `config_id` (Facebook Login for Business) instead of `scope`. The scopes are the six publishing permissions only; Meta Ads and WhatsApp are not requested and their catalog cards no longer borrow the Meta connection's status.
11. `POST /api/connect/salla {action:"sync_sales"}` (`store-sales.ts`, `connectors/salla.ts` `sallaSales`): reads `GET /orders` for the last 30 days (up to 10 pages) and `GET /carts/abandoned` (60 a page, up to 3 pages) with a fresh token, reduces them to totals (order count, revenue by currency without canceled or refunded orders, open carts and their value, top 10 products each), and stores them in `storesales:{scope}` and in the file `store-sales.md`. No customer name, phone, email or checkout link is kept. Records whose amount cannot be read are counted as `unread`. `memoryBlock` includes `store-sales.md`, so every generation sees the totals; the Reports page shows them with a Sync button when Salla is connected.
12. `POST /api/connect/shopify {action:"sync_sales"}` (`store-sales.ts`, `connectors/shopify.ts` `shopifySales`, shared totals in `connectors/sales.ts`): the same snapshot from Shopify's Admin GraphQL API on the connected `*.myshopify.com` host only (see step 13). `orders` (last 30 days, 100 a page, up to 5 pages, 5 line items each) asks for no customer, email, phone or address field; `ordersCount` gives the full count. `abandonedCheckouts` (`created_at` in the last 30 days `AND status:open`, up to 3 pages) is read for totals and line titles only, with `abandonedCheckoutsCount` for the full count; `carts.capped` is set only when the count is missing and more pages remain. Revenue uses `currentTotalPriceSet` (after partial refunds and edits) and leaves out canceled, `REFUNDED` and `VOIDED` orders. HTTP 403 or an `ACCESS_DENIED` error means the app lacks protected customer data access to orders and returns `orders_access_denied` without marking the connection for reconnecting; HTTP 401 does mark it. One store per workspace: the card shows the store last synced while it stays connected, else the first connected of Salla and Shopify.
13. Every Shopify call (`shopifyGql`) is a POST to `https://{shop}/admin/api/2026-07/graphql.json` with the stored token: products (`importShopify`, 50 active products), the blog article (`publishShopifyArticle`: `blogs(first: 1)` and `shop.name`, then `articleCreate` with `isPublished: false` and the shop as author), and the sales queries. The shop must match `SHOP_HOST` before any request. A `THROTTLED` answer is retried twice, 2 s apart. The queries were validated against the Admin schema Shopify ships in `@shopify/dev-mcp` (2025-10, the oldest supported version) and use no deprecated field. Shopify requires GraphQL for new public App Store apps; the REST Admin calls are gone.
14. `POST /api/connect/zid {action:"sync_sales"}` (`store-sales.ts`, `connectors/zid.ts` `zidSales`): the same snapshot from Zid's Merchant API on `api.zid.sa` only, with both Zid tokens and `Store-Id` (a connection without a store id asks the profile once). Shapes come from Zid's official Python SDK and its fixtures; what they do not establish is handled in code. Orders: `GET /managers/store/orders?payload_type=default&sort_by=desc` (the only list payload with products), 100 a page, up to 5 pages, with a `date_from` a day early and no `date_to` (a UTC `date_to` can be a day behind Riyadh and drop the current day's orders) — Zid does not document what the date filter applies to, nor whether `total_order_count` honours it, so every row is checked against the 30 days on `created_at` (read as Riyadh time; the zone is undocumented), paging stops at the first page that reaches past the window, at a short page, or at `total_order_count`, and the count is always the orders read, never `total_order_count`. When the page cap stops it with orders left, `orders.capped` is set and the count reads "or more". Orders are de-duplicated by id. Revenue parses the decimal-string `order_total` under the order's own currency (never a default) and leaves out orders whose status is canceled, reversed or being reversed, or whose payment was refunded or voided; `partially_reversed` still counts. A list answer without an `orders` array fails the sync. Carts: `GET /managers/store/abandoned-carts` (100 a page, up to 3 pages, `capped` past that; the pagination block is not documented, so a short page ends it) counts the carts open now (not limited to the 30 days, like Salla's), leaving out phase `completed` and counting a cart seen on two pages once, and sums `cart_total`; product names come from the detail view of the newest 20 carts (four at a time; a failed detail is skipped). Errors: 401 marks the connection for reconnecting; 403 on orders answers `zid_access_denied` without marking it (the app lacks the permission, which a new connection does not fix); 403 on any cart page keeps the orders, drops whatever carts were read and sets `carts.unavailable` (any other cart error fails the sync), which `store-sales.md`, the Reports card and Dana's STORE DATA show as unknown rather than zero. About 28 requests at most, under Zid's 60 a minute. No customer name, phone, email, cart link or WhatsApp text is read into the snapshot.
14b. `POST /api/connect/woocommerce {action:"sync_sales"}` (`store-sales.ts`, `connectors/woocommerce.ts` `wooSales`): the same snapshot from the store's REST API v3 on the owner's own host, through `safeFetch` with `httpsOnly` (the key and secret travel in a Basic header, so a plain-http store is refused in verify, import and sales alike: `unsafe_url:not_https`). `GET /wp-json/wc/v3/orders?per_page=100&orderby=date&order=desc&after=…&dates_are_gmt=true&_fields=id,status,total,currency,date_created_gmt,date_paid_gmt,line_items,refunds`, up to 5 pages, with `after` a day early; the 30 days are enforced on `date_created_gmt` (GMT, no zone mark) because whether a store honours `after` is not assumed, `X-WP-TotalPages` only stops the paging early, and the count is never taken from `X-WP-Total`. `_fields` keeps billing, shipping, customer id and payment details out of the response. Revenue is paid orders only (founder, 2026-10-05): `pending` and `on-hold` (WooCommerce's awaiting payment confirmation), and any order whose `date_paid_gmt` is present and empty, count as orders and are summed in `orders.pending`, not in revenue; `cancelled`, `refunded`, `failed`, `trash` and checkout drafts count in `canceled` and in no total; a partly refunded order counts its total minus its (negative) refunds, a fully refunded one counts as canceled; each order is summed in its own currency. Carts are `unavailable` (core WooCommerce keeps none): unknown, not zero. 401 and 403 are one error, `woo_access_denied` (WooCommerce answers 401 for a wrong key and for a key without access to orders), which asks for a new Read key; a key connection is not marked for reconnect. A body that is not a list is `woo unexpected_orders`.
14c. Twice a day (`GET /api/cron/stores` at 02:00 and 14:00 UTC, the cron bearer; `store-sales.ts` `syncStoreIfDue`, rules 623-627): for each active workspace, one store read like the button's: the store of its own snapshot while still connected, or its only connected store when it never synced; several stores and no snapshot is `choose` (the owner picks with the button), a connection marked for reconnect is skipped (`reconnect`), a snapshot under 10 hours old is left (`fresh`). A failure keeps the last good snapshot with its own date and writes `storesyncfail:{scope}` (`{at, provider, error}`, 14 days, a status or known code only); a 5xx or network error throws so the batch retries it once, a key without access or a 4xx answers `failed:{code}` without a retry. The note shows under the store card (`reports.store.syncFailed`) until a sync, scheduled or by the button, succeeds. Ads keep their own once-a-day read in `cron/daily` (`syncAdsIfDue`).
15. Dana's report tasks (`weekly_report`, `board_deck`, `dashboard_spec`) get `statsFor`: the workspace KPIs and, when `storesales:{scope}` holds a snapshot, a STORE DATA block from `salesDataBlock` in `store-sales.ts` — the snapshot as escaped JSON with its sync date and age, the rule that there is no previous period to compare with, a note when the totals cover only the latest orders, a stale-sync line past 7 days, and currency codes limited to three letters. The workspace block's "sales are NOT in this data" line is replaced by a pointer to STORE DATA only when a snapshot exists. Traffic and ad spend are likewise said to be missing only without SITE DATA and AD DATA (section 50 step 5, rule 730).

### 11a. Connecting and publishing through Composio (branch `claude/composio-api-install-1d5bfc`, rules 645-652)

Files: `src/lib/connectors/{composio,composio-catalog,via-composio}.ts`, the three connect routes, `publish.ts`, `targets.ts`, `account.ts`. Applies to a provider named in `COMPOSIO_PROVIDERS` (Meta, X, LinkedIn, TikTok); the others keep the flow above.

1. `GET /api/connect/{provider}/start?toolkit=` (same session and admin+ check): `toolkit` must be one `PROVIDER_TOOLKITS[provider]` lists (Meta: `instagram`, `facebook`), otherwise the provider's first. `composioConnect` asks Composio for a link for the workspace's opaque id (`composioUserId(scope)`) with `callbackUrl = ${APP_ORIGIN}/api/connect/{provider}/callback?state=` and `allowMultiple: true` (a reconnect holds the old account until step 2 revokes it). A link that is not https `*.composio.dev` deletes the pending account. Any failure redirects back to `/{lang}/app/integrations?error=` (`connect_failed`, `composio_rate_limited` or `provider_not_configured`), never raw JSON, and logs only `errorShape` (class, status, Composio code). Every toolkit is pinned to a dated version (`TOOLKIT_VERSION`): the SDK refuses to execute on "latest". The state record also holds `via:"composio"`, the toolkit and the pending account id; the browser never carries the id back.
2. Callback: state and cookie as above, no `code`. The account must be ACTIVE within 10 s and listed under this workspace's id, or it is deleted and `error=connect_failed`. `connectViaComposio` reads the profile with listed read tools only (Facebook Pages as `id,name`, never Page tokens; Instagram user; X username; LinkedIn author urn) and saves `conn:{scope}:{provider}` with `data[toolkit] = accountId` and `meta.via = "composio"`, merging a second Meta toolkit into the same record. A replaced account is revoked. If anything fails before the record is saved, the new account is deleted at Composio (`composioDiscard`), so it cannot stay ACTIVE there.
3. Publishing: `publishTask` sends the same task to `via-composio.ts` when the record is a Composio one. Every call goes through `composioWrite`/`composioRead`: the tool and its argument names must be in `composio-catalog.ts`, 600 calls per workspace per hour, 20 s timeout, and the result is shaped like the direct connectors' errors so `publish-once` holds an unknown result and reconnects on a refused token. LinkedIn and TikTok publish only this way; TikTok sends an image as a draft to the owner's inbox.
4. Disconnect and account deletion revoke every account at Composio first (rule 651).

### 11b. Drafts: WhatsApp templates, private YouTube uploads, email drafts (branch `claude/composio-api-install-1d5bfc`, rules 657-660)

Nothing in this section reaches a customer or the public; the owner finishes each draft in the platform's own tool.

1. Targets (`targets.ts`): an item with `channel: "whatsapp"` goes to `whatsapp`; one with `channel: "email"` to `brevo` and `klaviyo`; a social item whose `videoUrl` is our own Blob copy to `youtube`. WhatsApp and YouTube are targets only from a Composio record.
2. Gate: `gateTask(..., { publish: true, provider })` holds every placeholder as before, except that numeric `{{n}}` variables pass when the provider is `whatsapp` (`TEMPLATE_TARGETS` in `brand-rules.ts`): they are the template's variables. The publish route, the chat publish action and the inbox buttons pass the provider.
3. WhatsApp: connect reads the business account (`WHATSAPP_GET_BUSINESS_ACCOUNT_DETAILS`; none answers `no_whatsapp_business`). Publish builds a template with `whatsappTemplate` (variables renumbered 1..k with neutral examples, category MARKETING, language from the item) and calls `WHATSAPP_CREATE_MESSAGE_TEMPLATE`. Empty, over 1,024 characters or a variable at the start or end is refused before sending; Meta's immediate REJECTED is a refusal (`whatsapp 400`).
4. YouTube: connect lists the owner's channel (`YOUTUBE_LIST_CHANNELS`, `mine: true`; none answers `no_youtube_channel`). Publish stages the Blob clip with Composio (`composioStageFile`: our Blob URL only, 30 s) and calls `YOUTUBE_UPLOAD_VIDEO` with `privacyStatus: "private"`, which the catalog's `fixed` values enforce in `composio.ts`. The answer links YouTube Studio.
5. Email: Brevo reads the account's senders, takes the first active one (none: `brevo_no_sender`) and creates a campaign with name, subject, sender and HTML only (no recipients, no send time). Klaviyo creates a CODE template. The HTML is the item rendered by `render` (md.ts), whose links pass `safeHref` (rules 545, 694). Both use the stored API key on fixed hosts.
6. Errors: the refusals above are in `publish-once`'s REFUSED list; `whatsapp`, `youtube`, `brevo` and `klaviyo` errors carry the provider's status, so a 4xx is a refusal and a 5xx may have posted.

## 12. API-key connectors

Files: `src/app/api/connect/[provider]/key/route.ts`, `src/lib/connectors/{wordpress,woocommerce,keyed}.ts`.

1. `POST /api/connect/{provider}/key` with the provider's `keyFields` (`registry.ts:28-81`). Session and admin+ (`connectors`) required; provider must be `kind:"key"`.
2. Every declared field is required. `type:"url"` fields get `https://` prefixed when they do not start with `http`.
3. Verification is a live call to the user-supplied host: WordPress `/wp-json/wp/v2/users/me`, WooCommerce `/wp-json/wc/v3/products` (https only), Odoo `/jsonrpc`, PrestaShop `/api/products`, Telegram `getChat`, Brevo `/v3/account`, Klaviyo `/api/accounts/`. Failure: 400 `verify_failed` with a `detail` string derived from the remote response or fetch error.
4. On success the credentials are stored encrypted under `conn:{sc(u)}:{provider}`; `meta` (site, account name) is stored in clear.

## 13. MCP server and OAuth 2.1 authorization

Files: `src/app/api/mcp/route.ts`, `src/app/api/mcp/token/route.ts`, `src/lib/mcp.ts`, `src/lib/oauth.ts`, `src/app/api/oauth/*`, `src/app/oauth/authorize/page.tsx`, `next.config.ts`.

### Personal token
`GET /api/mcp/token` returns only `exists`; `POST` revokes then creates `nashr_` + 24 random bytes base64url, stores `mcptoken:{sha256}` and `mcptoken-of:{email}` → that sha256 with no TTL, and returns the token once (it cannot be read back, rules 552, 555); `DELETE` revokes the personal token only. All three are owner only (`mcp.token`, 2026-09-29): a member and a founder impersonating get 403. The slot is keyed by the acting login, which past the gate is the owner.

### OAuth 2.1
1. Discovery: `/.well-known/oauth-authorization-server` and `/.well-known/oauth-protected-resource` (rewrites). Issuer and endpoints derive from `APP_ORIGIN`.
2. Client registration: `POST /api/oauth/register` is open to anyone. Keeps up to 10 `http(s)` redirect URIs, issues `client_id`, and a `client_secret` only when `token_endpoint_auth_method = client_secret_post`. Rate limited to 30 per IP per hour (429 `slow_down`); no expiry.
3. Authorize: `/oauth/authorize?client_id&redirect_uri&response_type=code&code_challenge&code_challenge_method&state&scope`. Anonymous users are redirected to `/ar/app/login?next=...`. The page checks client, exact `redirect_uri` membership, `response_type=code`, presence of `code_challenge`; shows Allow/Deny with the client name supplied at registration.
4. Approve: `POST /api/oauth/approve` (form post, session required) re-validates client and redirect URI, requires `code_challenge_method=S256`, issues a 10 min single-use code bound to client, redirect URI, challenge, email and `scope` (free text from the request, default `noor`). Deny redirects with `error=access_denied`. Owner only (`oauth.approve`, 2026-09-29): a member and a founder impersonating get 403, and the consent page shows them no form, only who is signed in.
5. Token: `POST /api/oauth/token` (JSON or form; client auth via Basic, body secret, or none for public clients; a secret is compared with `sameSecret`; 60 requests per IP per 15 minutes, checked first, 429 `slow_down`). `authorization_code`: the code (stored as `oauthcode:{sha256(code)}`) is taken with GETDEL, so it is single use under concurrency (rule 549), then client id, redirect URI and PKCE S256 are verified. Issues access token `nashr_...` stored as `mcptoken:{sha256}` → `{email, grant}` for 30 days and refresh token `nashr_r_...` in `oauthrefresh:{sha256}` for 90 days; the grant `mcpgrant:{email}:{id}` holds both hashes.
6. Refresh: `grant_type=refresh_token` rotates: the old refresh record is taken with GETDEL (rule 548), the grant must exist and list it (rule 550), the grant is rewritten with SET XX (rule 551), and a new pair is issued; client id must match. The previous access token is deleted and also refused at once, because the grant no longer lists it. A refresh token with no grant is refused.
7. `GET /api/mcp/grants` lists the account's grants and `DELETE ?id=` or `?all=1` revokes (Revocation, below); both are owner only (`oauth.grants`) since 2026-09-29.

### MCP calls
`POST /api/mcp` JSON-RPC. `initialize`, `ping`, `notifications/initialized`, `tools/list` answer without authentication (`mcp/route.ts:63-65`). Everything else needs `Authorization: Bearer nashr_...` resolved by `userFromBearer` (`src/lib/mcp.ts:36`): owner accounts only (a token naming a member login is refused), a suspended account refused, `scope` set from the owner's active brand like a browser session, actor `{kind:"token", role:"owner"}`. Every tool is mapped to an action (`MCP_TOOL_ACTION` in `src/lib/mcp.ts`: `ask_noor` `chat`, `generate_content` `generate`, `list_inbox`, `read_file`, `list_files`, `get_brand_rules` and `check_brand` `read`; `save_draft` `drafts`); an unmapped tool is refused (-32602) and so is one `can()` refuses. Tools: `ask_noor` (full chat loop with all fifteen chat tools, including `publish_item`, Reem's five to-do tools and `handoff_note`; the loop runs with `via: "mcp"`, so `runTool` refuses publishing, core-memory writes, `import_catalog` and handoff notes; since 2026-10-01 it passes the same gates as the web chat (section 7 step 14) before `chat()` saves anything: the message trimmed and cut to 4,000 characters, an empty one a tool error ("Empty message. Nothing was sent.") that counts nothing; the burst limit (a tool error); then the account's one monthly allowance, shared with the app (`reserveChatTurn`), whose refusal is a tool error in plain words (`refusalText`: the messages used, when they renew, that nothing was sent, and what the owner can do on the Subscription page). The turn then runs with the request's deadline, the per-message ceiling and the history guard, and is settled like a web turn; when 10% or less of the allowance is left the answer ends with `[chat: N of M messages left this month]` ("in the trial" on a trial), and a message that continued on credits ends with `[chat: this month's M messages are used; this message continued on credits and was billed N credits]`, `generate_content` (charges credits through `generateBatch`, section 8 step 13, with `by` = `tokenBy(user)`; trial expiry is checked, section 8 step 8), `list_inbox`, `read_file`, `list_files`, `get_brand_rules` (the active brand's tone, dialect, audience, products, offers, preferred words, channels and the enforced banned words as JSON; free), `check_brand` (`{text, type?}`, up to 20,000 characters, checked with the same `gateTask` as the approve button; returns `pass`, `violations` and a summary; internal types are not held; free, no model call), `save_draft` (below).

### MCP `save_draft` (2026-09-29): drafts from the owner's own assistant
Lets an owner write with their own Claude or ChatGPT subscription and still review in Nashr. `{type, body, title?, channel?, notes?, lang?}`, one item per call (`src/lib/external-drafts.ts`; action `drafts`, any role, and an MCP bearer is always the owner):
1. `parseDraft` validates before anything is read or written: `type` must be in `TASK_TYPES` (`isTaskType`, so `constructor` and friends fail); `body` is required, at most 20,000 characters; `title` at most 200 (defaults to the body's first line, 80 characters); `notes` at most 500 (kept as `brief`); `channel` a lowercase `[a-z0-9_-]{1,30}` name (defaults to the type); `lang` `ar` or `en` (defaults to the brand's first language). Control and bidi-override characters are stripped. A field over its limit is refused with a tool error, never truncated. Any other argument (`scope`, `email`, `id`) is ignored.
2. `draftAllowed(user.email)` (`rate.ts`): 30 an hour and 150 a day per account, across its brands. Over the limit: tool error, nothing saved.
3. The brand of `sc(u)` (the bearer's active brand, set by `userFromBearer`) is read; without one: tool error, nothing saved.
4. The brand gate runs in code (`gateTask`, the approve and publish routes' check). Status is `initialStatus(false, ...)`, i.e. always `pending`: the owner's auto-approval does not apply to text the owner has not seen in Nashr. A draft that breaks a rule is saved anyway, held like an agent's draft (the inbox names the rule, `PATCH /api/tasks/{id}` refuses to approve it with 409, publish refuses it), and the tool answer returns `brandCheck.violations` so the assistant can fix and save again. This matches every other creation path, which keeps a violating item pending rather than dropping it.
5. `saveTask(sc(u), task, true)` with `source:"external"` (ad and social types get an ad code there, as on every path). No credits, no model call, no judge, no cross-agent review (section 33; the inbox card says "unreviewed · written outside Thakeen"), no trial-expiry check (like `check_brand`, it spends nothing).
6. Answer: JSON with `saved`, `id`, `status`, `type`, `channel`, `title`, `brandCheck` and `credits: 0`. 401 responses carry `WWW-Authenticate` pointing at the resource metadata. CORS is `*`.

## 14. Founder console

Files: `admin/page.tsx`, `src/components/admin/{shared,Overview,Users,Usage,Billing,Quality,Log,AdminNav}.tsx` (one component per section, rule 874), `src/components/app/{AdminLogin,GoldenPanel,WishlistPanel,LeadsPanel,PartnersPanel,PaymentsPanel}.tsx`, `src/lib/admin-fetch.ts`, `src/app/api/admin/*` (including `admin/media`), `src/lib/auth.ts:321-500`, `src/lib/admin-stats.ts` (`overview`, `renderSpend`).

1. Page access: `isAdminCookie()`; otherwise 404, or the key form when `?login=1` (`admin/page.tsx:15-18`). `isAdminCookie()` accepts a live key session or a founder signed in as themselves (`isFounderActor`, 2026-09-29): not a member of a founder's account, not a founder impersonating.
2. Key login: `POST /api/admin/login {key}`; 5 attempts per 15 min per IP; `sameSecret` compare; opens a key session (`adminsession:{sha256(id)}` with a fingerprint of the key, `at` and `seen`) and sets `nashr_admin` to the random id. The session ends after 15 minutes without an admin request and 12 hours after the key entry at most; `adminKeySession` renews it on each admin request, and `keyImpLive` on each request through a customer impersonation it opened (one `SET XX EX`, at most once a minute), so the idle window counts from the last renewal (14 to 15 minutes after the last request). Once it ends, the console's next admin call is 401 and the console replaces itself with a bilingual "key session ended" message linking to `?login=1` (or to the sign-in page for a founder session), never an empty user table (`adminFetch` in `src/lib/admin-fetch.ts`, rule 436). `DELETE` (the console's "End key session" button) deletes the record and the cookie (no auth needed). Rotating `WAITLIST_ADMIN_KEY` ends every key session.
3. Data: `GET /api/admin/overview`, `/plan` (all users with usage, and since 2026-10-01 each account's chat: `chatUsed`, `chatAllowance`, `chatLeft`, `chatPeriod`, `chatOverride`, `chatOverflowOn`, `chatOverflowUsed`, `chatCredits`, `chatUsd` and `usdPerMsg`, read with `chatStatus` beside the usage in the same `pmap` row, so a trial's count is its trial count; `chatUsed` is the raw count `ChatStatus.counted`, which can pass an allowance lowered mid-period, `chatOverflowOn` is the status's (off on a trial), `chatCredits` what the messages continued on credits were billed this month, and `usdPerMsg` is `chatUsdPerMessage` in `src/lib/admin-stats.ts`: this month's chat cost over every message sent, `counted` plus the messages on credits, null when none was), `/users/{email}` (user without `passHash`, default-scope brand, tasks of all brands, usage, the account's `chat` status since 2026-10-01 with `counted` and `overflow.credits`, and `chatUsdPerMsg`, the list's cost per message; connections metadata), `/log` (last 50), `/evals`, `/golden`, `/export` (CSV of all users), `/media` (the media provider check, step 9). The user detail pane shows the model cost per kind of request and, since 2026-09-29, per agent (`getUsage().byAgent`: calls and dollars for the work each agent did, onboarding counted as Noor's), and, after the tasks list, the latest 100 activity rows of each brand (`listActivity` per scope from the account's own brand list, in one `Promise.all`; section 32 step 6), as text.
4. Actions via `POST /api/admin/plan {email, plan?, deal?, addCredits?, seats?, chatAllowance?, videoGrant?, reason?}` and `POST /api/admin/users/{email} {action}`: `suspend`, `unsuspend`, `extendTrial` (1..90 days), `resetPassword` (returns a temporary password once), `setLang`, `impersonate`, `delete` (`wipeAccount`). All write to `admin:log` except `setLang`. The log `actor` is the founder's email or `"key"` (`adminActor`, 2026-09-29); `POST /api/admin/golden` still writes `"founder"`. `POST /api/admin/plan` also takes `seats` (an integer 1..500 sets `User.seats`, `null` or 0 clears it, anything else 400 `bad_seats`) and runs `enforceSeats` after a plan or seat change (section 26 step 13). Since 2026-09-30 (rules 243-244): `plan` is one of trial, starter, pro, business, agency; a plan change sets the balance to `allowanceOf(u)` and restarts the renewal cycle; `deal: {credits, priceSar}` sets an Agency contract (400 `bad_deal` or `below_floor` under 999 SAR, 1,500 credits or the Business rate; 400 `deal_needs_agency` on any other plan, nothing saved), `deal: null` or leaving agency clears it, and with it the seat override that holds the deal's seats unless the same request sends `seats` (logged as `seats:plan`, and the team is refitted), and a deal change alone keeps the balance until the next renewal; `addCredits` must be a finite number of at most 100,000 either way (400 `bad_credits`); the log's before and after carry the deal. Since 2026-10-01 (rule 316): `chatAllowance` sets the founder's override of the plan's chat messages (a whole number 0..100,000, `isChatOverride`; 0 stops chat for the account), `null` or `""` clears it back to the plan's, anything else is 400 `bad_chat` before any read or write; it is written under the user lock in the same save, dropped like the seat override when the contract ends (leaving Agency, or clearing the deal) unless the same request sets it (logged `chat:plan`), and logged as `chat:N` with `chat` in the before and after. The console's user table has a Chat column ("used / allowance", "(override)", "+N on credits", red at 10% or less left and at 0; the tooltip gives this month's chat cost, the cost per message and the owner's switch) whose number opens a prompt for the override, checked with `isChatOverride` before it is sent; the detail pane shows the messages used of the allowance for the period, the messages on credits, the switch ("not offered on a trial") and the chat cost; the users CSV has a `chat_allowance` column. The console lists plans from `PLANS`, shows an Agency row's deal behind an "Agency deal…" control ("no deal: Business" without one) and sets it from prompts for credits, price and seats, pre-checked with `parseDeal`; an account's first deal is sent with `plan: "agency"` so its allowance applies at once, and a change to an existing deal asks whether to apply it now (the same) or at the next renewal (the deal alone). Its Paying and Trial tiles count live accounts only, like MRR; the console's user table shows each account's seat limit (marked when overridden), sets the override from a prompt, and reports who was paused or came back and how many invites were cancelled. `resetPassword` also stamps `teamSessionsAfter`, signing every member out. Member logins never enter `users:all`, so the console looks an exact address up instead: `GET /api/admin/users/{email}` answers `{ok, kind:"member", member:{email, name, role, memberOf, invitedBy, acceptedAt, disabled, ownerExists, onRoster}}` for a member login, and `POST /api/admin/users/{email} {action:"removeMember", reason?}` deletes it (`forceRemoveMember`, under the same locks as any removal; logged), which frees the address, for example one taken through a hand-passed invite link. Every other action on a member address is 400 `bad_action`. Since 2026-10-01 (rules 446-448, section 38): the account's card has a Top-ups block (each live pack: credits left of the pack, expiry, what was paid and on which date, the payment reference and who activated it) with "Add top-up" (prompts for the pack, the amount paid excluding VAT, the payment date and the reference, then `POST /api/admin/topups {action:"add", paymentId}` through `adminFetch`; disabled on a trial; it also records the pack's payment in the Payments panel, with the partner's commission when one is due, rule 454, so the founder never records a top-up there) and "Void" per pack (`{action:"void", id}`, after a confirmation); the users list shows each balance's share from packs (`topupCredits` on the `/plan` rows) and "+ credits" now adds goodwill credits to the month's plan credits only; the Overview and Billing tabs show "Top-ups this month" (`overview().revenue.topupSarMonth`) next to MRR, never inside it. Since 2026-10-08 (rule 783): `videoGrant: {until, note?}` opens every video model to the account through the end of the UTC day `until` (a real day after today and at most 366 days ahead, a note of at most 200 characters; anything else is 400 `bad_grant` before the account is read), `null` clears it; a plan change keeps it; it is logged as `video:all@{until}` or `video:plan`, with `video` in the before and after, and each `GET /api/admin/plan` row carries `videoGrant`. Since 2026-10-08 (rule 784) the account's detail has an entitlements table (credits, seats, chat month, chat rate, video models, agents, active and ends, each with the plan's value, the override that differs from it, the effective value, what is used and when it resets) and a "Video grant" button beside "Extend trial": it prompts for the UTC day (empty clears the grant, and sends nothing when the account has none) and a note, sends `videoGrant` to `POST /api/admin/plan`, shows the server's `bad_grant` as the other actions show their errors and reads the detail again.
5. Impersonation: saves the caller's current `nashr_session` into `nashr_return` (2 h), sets `nashr_impersonate`, then `createSession(target, 2 h, {imp: who})`: the session record names the founder (or `"key"`) and resolves to actor kind `founder`, which is refused account deletion, password change, every team change, the MCP token, OAuth approval and the console itself (`permissions.md` 0.1). Logged with `expiresInMin:120` and the acting founder; opened from a key session it also ends with that key session (15 minutes without a request on either side, 12 hours at most), logged as `idleMin:15, endsWithKeySession:true` (rule 435).
6. Return: `POST /api/admin/impersonate` (form post from the banner in `app/layout.tsx`). No admin check; rate limit 30 per IP per 15 min first (429). The caller's `nashr_session` record is deleted when it is an impersonation session or `nashr_impersonate` is present; if `nashr_return` names a live session it becomes `nashr_session`, else `nashr_session` is deleted. Both helper cookies are cleared. `impersonate:stop` is logged only when the burned record carried `imp`, with `actor` = that `imp` and `email` = the record's email, never the cookie value (rules 540-542).
7. `POST /api/admin/golden {agent?, resume?}` runs the golden set (up to 300 s). `GET /api/admin/golden` also returns `hashes`, the prompt versions deployed now (section 31), and the Golden panel marks an agent whose prompts changed since the previous run with a "prompt changed" badge.
8. Render cost (Billing section, `src/components/admin/Billing.tsx`; spec 4.9). `overview()` (`src/lib/admin-stats.ts`) fetches `renderSpend()` in the same `Promise.all` as `getUsage("_all")`; `renderSpend(month = monthKey())` reads `render:_all:{month}:m:{model}:{jobs|failed|credits|usd_micro}` for all 11 catalogue models and `render:_all:{month}:kie_centicredits` in one `Promise.all` (45 reads, no loop awaits) and sums the provider and month totals in code (`usd = usd_micro / 1e6`, `kieCredits = centicredits / 100`). `Overview.revenue` gains `renderCostUsd`, `renderByProvider` (`{usd, jobs, failed}` per provider) and `renderByModel` (`{jobs, failed, credits, usd}` per model). The gross margin is now `(MRR − (model USD + render USD) × 3.75) / MRR`, and "credits used this month" counts `t.mediaCredits ?? (t.image ? IMAGE_COST : 0)` per item. The tab shows a fifth tile "Render cost this month $", a "Per model" table (model, done, failed, credits kept, USD, $ per credit), one line per provider, and a "Check media providers" button.
9. `GET /api/admin/media` (`src/app/api/admin/media/route.ts`, `isAdmin` else 401, `maxDuration = 60`, `dynamic = "force-dynamic"`), behind that button: one `Promise.all` of `kieAdapter.check` (the kie balance) when kie is configured, `higgsfieldAdapter.check` (`/estimate` per model with its real body, plus the Seedance text-to-video body) when Higgsfield is, `probeBlob` (write, check and delete a tiny public file), the sizes of the three open sets, `renderSpend()`, `offModels()` and `mjobs:recent` (0..49); each check is bounded by a 10 s `abortAfter` and a throw becomes `{error: "unreachable"}`. Answer: `{ok, configured: {fal, kie, higgsfield, blob, webhooks}, modelsOn, available, blob, kie, higgsfield, open: {fal, kie, higgsfield}, spend, off, recent}`, where `kie` and `higgsfield` are `null` when not configured, `webhooks` is `!!callbackUrl("kie")`, and `recent` rows keep only `{id, model, state, error, providerCredits, costUsd, at}`. Flags only: no variable value is ever in the answer (the `MEDIA_MODELS_ON` ids are not secret). The console prints it as plain text, with a false flag, `webhooks: off`, a Blob probe that is not `ok`, an error string and each switched-off model in red.
10. Leads tab (2026-10-01, #134; `src/components/app/LeadsPanel.tsx`, `src/lib/leads.ts`): reads `GET /api/admin/leads`, enterprise and early-adopter requests (support tickets from `ContactForm`, whose "Name:" and "Company:" lines are lifted out) and affiliate applications, newest first, 50 per page, with a toggle to every support ticket and a CSV export (`csvSafe`). Opening the tab posts `{action: "seen", until}` with the time of the newest lead it showed; the server stores that time (clamped to now, never moved backwards) in `admin:leads:seen`, so a lead that arrived between the load and the post stays unread, and the tab label shows how many sales leads are newer. A warning shows while `mailReady()` is false (new leads may not reach the founder's inbox) or `WAITLIST_TO` is unset; both say that affiliate applications are never emailed and appear only in this tab. Everything shown is visitor text, rendered as text.
11. Cost per model and provider (Billing tab, 2026-10-09; section 8 item 15.8). `GET /api/admin/overview` (`isAdmin` first, 401 otherwise) carries `modelUsage`: this month's calls and USD per catalog model (and `other`) and per provider from `getUsage("_all")`, the requests re-sent to Anthropic after a gateway failure, and the customer picks the router refused, keyed `purpose:reason`. The tab lists the models by cost with USD per call, the providers, the fallback count and the refused picks, with one muted line saying the USD is at catalog (list) rates and that a request re-sent to Anthropic counts under `straitly`; the block renders nothing when the answer has no `modelUsage`. No new route, and nothing in it reaches a customer.

## 15. Referrals

Files: `src/lib/referral.ts`, `src/lib/partners.ts` (`recordPayment` → `rewardReferrer`), `src/app/api/referral/route.ts`, `src/app/api/referral/share/route.ts`, `refer/page.tsx`, signup route.

1. Code = first 8 alphanumerics of base64url SHA-256 of `"nashr-ref:" + email`, uppercased; stored at `ref:{CODE}`.
2. At signup with a valid `ref` (rule 556): the new account gets +`REFERRAL_CREDITS` (50) through `adjust`; `referrals:{referrer}` gets an entry, `referredBy:{new}` is set, and `referral:pending:{referrer}` (hash, field = a SHA-256 of the new address, value `{at, status: "pending"}`) records the referrer's bonus as pending. The referrer gets nothing at signup. Self-referral is blocked by email equality only.
3. Referrer bonus (rules 557-558, 2026-10-05): after `recordPayment` (`src/lib/partners.ts`) records a payment that stands (plan or top-up), and on a retry of the same payment id, `rewardReferrer(payer)` reads `referredBy:{payer}`, claims the referred account with one `SADD referral:paid:{referrer}` (only the caller that added it continues, so a bonus is decided once per referred account whatever payments follow, are retried, race or are voided; the claim is never released), then `INCRBY referral:month:{referrer}:{YYYY-MM}` (Riyadh month, `EXPIRE NX` 62 days). Above `REFERRAL_MONTHLY_CAP` (10) the pending record becomes `capped` and nothing is paid; otherwise `adjust(referrer, 50)` and `paid`. It runs outside the payer's lock; a failure is logged and never fails the payment. No clawback when that payment is later voided.
4. `GET /api/referral` returns code, count (sign-ups), pending (bonuses waiting for a first payment), earned (paid bonuses plus referrals from before 2026-10-05, which were paid at signup) and the list of referred emails with timestamps. Owner only (`referral`, 2026-09-29), as is `/api/referral/share` (step 5); the refer page shows other roles a note and reads nothing.
5. `POST /api/referral/share {platform}` (x, linkedin, whatsapp, instagram): +20 credits once per platform. A legacy per-platform key `shared:{email}:{platform}` counts as claimed; otherwise the claim is one atomic `SADD` to `shared:{email}` and only the caller that added the platform is paid, so parallel requests cannot each take the bonus (`share/route.ts:25-28`, since 2026-09-20). No proof of sharing is required.

## 16. Scheduled jobs

See `architecture.md` 2.6 and 4(d). Details:

- Daily (`src/app/api/cron/daily/route.ts`): per (user, active brand): `renewIfDue` (since 2026-10-01 it resets the month's plan credits only; top-up packs keep what is left in them); on the default brand's entry, since 2026-10-01, `saveSettledTopups` when `getUser` took an expired top-up pack out of the balance (`topupsDirty`), which writes the settled record under the ledger lock (outcome `topups_expired`, section 38 step 6); on the default brand's entry, since 2026-09-30, `sweepOnboardHolds` (an added brand's setup charge that no run charged or reused for a day is refunded when its brand does not exist and dropped when it does; outcome `onboard_refunded:n` / `onboard_settled:n`, and one `onboard_refunded` activity row in each refunded brand, section 32 step 2); on the same entry, since 2026-10-01, `sweepWeeklyHolds` (`src/lib/schedule.ts`, rule 337: a Sunday-plan hold `User.paidOnce[weekly:{scope}:{week}]` of a week that has ended and taken over 10 minutes ago is refunded when that week has no `weekly_plan` task the team made on its own (`isAutonomous`) in its brand, else dropped; after the renewal, so a refund adds to the new month's balance; outcome `weekly_refunded:n` / `weekly_settled:n`, `weekly_sweep_failed:...` on an error, which never stops the rest of the day); then, for every scope with or without a brand, `secretaryDaily` purges Reem's items deleted over 30 days ago and closes items whose linked inbox item was approved (section 25 steps 7 and 8); if no brand and account older than 10 min and `mailReady()` (key and `MAIL_FROM`, since 2026-10-01), a one-time nudge email through `sendOnce` (`notified:{email}:nudge1`, written only after Resend accepted it; a refusal is reported as `mail_refused:nudge` and retried on the next run; a marker the old claim-before-send guard wrote, which proves no delivery, is cleared once per account and message when `mailReady()`, the message is due and the marker was written before `REPAIR_BEFORE` (read from its remaining TTL, so a marker written after MAIL_FROM went live is never cleared whatever the deploy order), then sent: founder decision 2026-10-01, `notifyrepair:` flag); else `runLifecycle` (day 1, 2, 3, 10 emails, once each, same `sendOnce` guard and the same one-time repair of an old marker while the note's window lasts, nothing without `mailReady()`), `runDaily` (brief stored at `brief:{scope}`, no model call; since 2026-09-29 with Reem's block, section 25 step 9), `followUps` (notifications with fingerprints; Reem's overdue follow-up is number 6. The brand-level ones, `due:{day}` and `pending:{n}`, and the Sunday plan's `weekly:{day}` go through `brandFingerprint(scope, …)`: an added brand's carries its brand id, the default brand's is unchanged, so every brand of a multi-brand account is nudged once, and on an account with more than one active brand the title names the brand (`brandTag`, `withBrand`). `credits` and `trial` stay once per account (#15)), and an email according to `profile:{email}.emails` (`all|important|none`; "important" also sends when a to-do is overdue or due today). Then, for every idle scope (an archived brand, and every brand of a suspended account), the same batch runs only the purge (`purgeIdle`), so a deleted item's text never outlives its 30 days there either. Links in the nudge and brief emails use `appOrigin()` (src/lib/origin.ts: `APP_ORIGIN`, else the one production fallback).
- Weekly (`src/app/api/cron/weekly/route.ts`, `schedule.ts` `runWeekly`): charges `TASK_COST.weekly_plan` (2 credits since 2026-09-30) to Noor. `weeklyPlan` is on by default only for the default brand; an added brand's is off until the owner switches it on (`getSchedule`, rule 247), and `saveSchedule` stores only fields set explicitly, so the daily run's `lastDaily` write never freezes a default. Records written before 2026-09-30 hold the old default; since the owner's switch stamps `weeklyPlanSetAt`, an unstamped stored `weeklyPlan` on an added brand is ignored, so those brands start off too. Skips when no brand, `weeklyPlan` off, trial over, weekly cap reached (`gov.weeklyCap`, counts the items made on their own in 7 days, `isAutonomous` in `brand.ts`: `source:"scheduled"`, the routines' included, and the items of Majed's auto-run, section 35; not an MCP `save_draft` draft), Noor's monthly credit cap reached in that brand (`agent_cap`) or insufficient credits (`no_credits`); the batch itself goes through `generateBatch` (section 8 step 13, `via:"weekly"`), which reads Noor's preferences, memory, data block and feedback inside the refunding try. Since 2026-10-01 (issues #29, #31): `generateBatch` renews a paid account due its monthly credits before the charge, so on a Sunday the 1st the 03:00 plan no longer meets last month's balance before the 05:00 daily renewal (rule 332); the charge goes through `chargeOnce` under `weeklyClaim(scope, weekStartUtc())`, so a run the platform killed after its charge leaves a hold the retry reuses and one week is never paid twice (rule 333); and the task saves and the `lastWeekly` marker (`opts.commit`) are inside the refund guard, so a failure before the marker refunds once and drops the hold, while a retry after the marker stops at `ranThisWeek` (rule 334). Tasks saved before a failed save or marker are removed with the refund (rule 334). A run killed with no retry in its week keeps its hold until the daily cron of a later week resolves it (`sweepWeeklyHolds`, rule 337): refunded when that week has no `weekly_plan` task the schedule made, dropped when it has one (the run saved its plan before it was killed: the charge stands). A run killed after its saves and before the marker leaves its plan in the inbox, so the retry of that week reuses the hold and plans again: two plans, one charge. Response body maps account email to result string.
- Golden (`cron/golden/route.ts`, `golden.ts`): fixed fictional brands, meters usage under `golden@nashr.internal`, notifies founder accounts on regressions. Each run records its prompt versions and tags a regression line "(prompt changed)" when that agent's prompts changed since the previous run (section 31). It never runs the cross-agent review (`crossReview: false`, section 33): the review is notes only, never changes what is scored, and would cost a call per reviewer per case.
- Media (`cron/media/route.ts`, `20 5 * * *`, `maxDuration = 300`): `sweepMedia(mediaDeps(), {deadline})` with no job limit; section 23 step 9. Not a step of the daily cron, whose per-workspace budget cannot hold an advance that may take most of a minute.
- Opportunistic media sweep: the image, video and poll routes schedule `maybeSweep` in `after()`; it runs `sweepMedia` with at most 6 jobs, only when it wins `msweep` (300 s) and at least 30 s of the request's deadline are left.
- Routines (`src/app/api/cron/routines/route.ts`, `src/lib/routines.ts`, 2026-09-29), daily at 06:00 UTC, after the 05:00 daily job has renewed credits. Four routines, each for an agent with real input data: `dana_weekly_report` (Dana, `weekly_report`, weekly), `noor_cart_recovery` (Noor, `cart_recovery`, weekly), `razan_cash_forecast` (Razan, `cash_forecast`, monthly) and `majed_monthly_review` (Majed, `strategy_review`, monthly). The other agents have none until they have a data source. All are off by default; the owner turns each on per brand on the Scheduled tasks page (`PUT /api/schedule {routines: {id: boolean}}`, only known ids with boolean values, merged per id into `schedule:{scope}.routines`). There is no "Run now" for a routine.
  1. Items: `dueRoutines(allUsers())` reads every (user, active brand) schedule in parallel, then the last runs of the routines that are on, and keeps those that are on and have not run in their period (the UTC week from Sunday, `weekStartUtc`, or the UTC month, `monthKey`). `runCronBatch` with job `routines`, period = today's UTC date, id `{scope}:{routineId}`, four at a time, no new item after 160 s, markers for 2 days. One item is one generation, and it needs 180 s of the invocation left before its hold and charge (`BATCH_RUN_MIN_MS`, measured from the route's `deadline`, the invocation's start plus 300 s), else it answers `retry:late` at once and charges nothing; a hop that ran something and left late items hands them to a fresh invocation (`RETRY_LATE` in `cron-batch.ts`: unlike `retry:locked` and `retry:paused`, which would only spin the chain, a late item starts in the next hop's first wave). A hop in which every item it took was late does not chain. The same batch also carries Majed's auto-run items (`dueHandoffs`, at most one per brand scope, id `{scope}:auto_handoff`, listed first; section 35). A missed day needs no catch-up route: the next day's run asks again and the period check decides. The batch's attempt count is per day, so charges left by killed runs are bounded by the runner's own count per routine and period (step 4).
  2. `runRoutine(user, id, deadline)` in this order: brand (`no_brand`); switched on (`off`); not yet run in its period (`routinelast:{scope}:{id}`, `already_this_period`); model state (`retry:paused` while paused, `skip:mock` without a key, since placeholders must never be charged); lock `routinelock:{scope}:{id}` (300 s, `retry:locked`); trial (`trial_over`); the last run read again under the lock; the gate; the invocation's time left (`retry:late` with less than 180 s, before the hold, the attempt count and the charge: a batch the platform kills after its charge keeps it and saves nothing); the weekly cap and the batch's hold; the attempt count (`attempts_exhausted`); the charge. The hold is deleted and the lock released in a `finally`, after the batch is saved.
  3. Gates, checked in code; a gate that fails answers `idle` or `no_data`, charges nothing and is final for the day (the next day's run asks again). Only Noor's gate asks for data newer than the routine's last run; Razan's and Majed's look at a fixed window, so they can run again in the next month on the same sync or decisions. Dana: an item the owner created or any decision in the last 7 days (light index `createdAt`/`decidedAt`, newest decision record), or a store sync (`storesales:{scope}.at`), an ad-account read (`adsync:{scope}:{provider}`) or a person's Refresh of the site or social figures (`ownersync:{scope}`, section 50 step 9; the daily cron's site and social reads do not count, rule 736) in the last 7 days; items made on their own (`isAutonomous`: the weekly plan, a routine or Majed's auto-run) do not count, or the team would keep itself busy for an absent owner; a handoff item a login ran, and an MCP draft, do. Noor: abandoned-cart data (`missingData` is null, else `no_data`) synced after the routine last ran (else `idle`). Razan: a store sales snapshot from the last 35 days (else `no_data`). Majed: decisions on at least 3 different items in the last 30 days, from the decision records or the light index's `decidedAt` (else `idle`).
  4.
