Weekly cap: the items made on their own in the last 7 days (`isAutonomous`: `source:"scheduled"` and Majed's auto-run items), plus the batches the brand's other routines or its auto-run hold right now (`routinehold:{scope}:{id}` and `handoffhold:{scope}` = their `TASK_COUNT`), plus this batch's `TASK_COUNT` must not pass `gov.weeklyCap` (`cap_reached`); the weekly plan, the routines and the auto-run share it (`holdBatch(scope, holdKey, count, cap)`). The cron runs a brand's routines side by side, so this check and setting the routine's own hold (360 s) happen under a short lock per brand (`routinecap:{scope}`, waited for up to about 2 s, else `retry:locked`); holds are read before the items, and a run deletes its hold only after its batch is saved (in `runRoutine`'s `finally`), so every batch is counted once in one of the two. Then the attempt count `routinetry:{scope}:{id}:{period}` (8 or 35 days) is raised before the charge and lowered again when the charge is refused or refunded; a platform kill leaves it raised, and once two such charges are left in the period the routine answers `attempts_exhausted` (final for the day, nothing charged) until its next period. Then `charge(email, TASK_COST[type], {scope, agent, cap: gov.agentCaps[agent]})` (`agent_cap`, `no_credits`) and `warnAgentCap`.
  5. The charge, the refund and the save go through `generateBatch` (section 8 step 13, `via:"routine"`, with the routine's attempt as `settle` and the invocation's `deadline`, which bounds the cross-agent review of Majed's monthly review, section 33). Inside the refunding try, one round: `listPreferencesFor`, `memoryBlock`, `statsFor(type)` (Dana and Majed: WORKSPACE DATA, plus STORE DATA when a store was synced; Noor: CART DATA; Razan: STORE DATA) and `feedbackFor`; then `generate` with the routine's note. A throw refunds with the charge's receipt, lowers the attempt count and is rethrown (the cron counts a failed attempt).
  6. The batch is saved in one parallel round with `source:"scheduled"` and `brief` = the routine id, judged with the same grounding (`judgeLater`), `routinelast:{scope}:{id}` is set, and one `pending` notification goes to the account (fingerprint `routine:{scope}:{id}:{period}`, 8 or 35 days). The answer is `ok:N`.
  7. The outcome of every run that took the lock is recorded in the activity log (`recordRun`, section 32 step 3); the Sunday plan's the same way, `via:"weekly"`.
- Users can trigger their own runs: `POST /api/schedule {run:"weekly"|"daily"}`. Any login may (the run is charged to the account); turning routines or the weekly plan on or off (`PUT /api/schedule`) needs admin+ (`schedule.write`). A weekly Run now passes the acting login's `byOf(u)`, so its rows name who clicked it (the owner, a member or the founder through support access); the cron's are the system's.

## 17. Support and contact tickets

`POST /api/support {email, message, type?, page?, lang?}` (`src/app/api/support/route.ts`): anonymous allowed; rate limit 10 per IP per hour; when a session exists the ticket records the acting login as `user` (a member's own address) and the account's email as `account`; pushes to `support-tickets`; emails `WAITLIST_TO` when set. `GET` (admin) returns the latest 500. Tickets have `status:"open"` and no update path. `POST /api/integrations` records `email` and `account` the same way.

`POST /api/affiliates` stores applications the same way (`affiliate-applications`), `GET` is admin only. Since 2026-10-01 both lists are read by the founder console's Leads tab (section 14).

## 18. Waitlist

`src/app/api/waitlist/route.ts`. `POST {email, company?, industry?, lang?}`: anonymous; dedupes on `waitlist:emails` through the shared store (`db`, 2026-10-01: memory in development; in a production deployment without Redis the request fails instead of pretending to store); the record is never logged (it holds an email address, rule 432). `GET [?format=csv]`: `isAdmin(req)`, like every admin route (the `x-admin-key` header, a key session or a founder session; never `?key=`). No UI in `src/` posts to this route at present.

## 19. Launch journey (milestones, levels, streak)

Files: `src/lib/journey.ts` (pure: `computeJourney`, `levelFor`, `decisionStreak`; the browser imports it), `src/lib/journey-load.ts` (server: one parallel round), `src/components/app/JourneyCard.tsx`; shown on the inbox (`app/page.tsx`) and at the top of the chat thread until complete (`chat/page.tsx`).

1. Seven milestones, each read from the workspace, never from a counter the interface keeps: `brand` (brand file exists), `guard` (at least one banned word), `approve` (an item approved by the owner, i.e. with `decidedAt`; auto-approved items do not count), `schedule` (any `scheduledFor`), `connect` (any connection), `publish` (any item with a live publish), `ai` (a personal MCP token or an OAuth grant).
2. Levels by milestones done: seed 0, sapling 2, palm 4, oasis 7. Points per milestone sum to 150. Points and levels are cosmetic, are not credits and buy nothing; the card says so.
3. Streak: consecutive UTC days, ending today or yesterday, with at least one owner decision (`decidedAt`).
4. Celebration: the card compares the done set and level with `localStorage` key `nashr:journey:v1` and plays confetti and a toast once per new milestone (level change louder). First visit in a browser celebrates only `brand`. Storage failures are ignored. The inbox dispatches `nashr:milestone` after a successful approve, date or publish so the card updates before the next server render. Motion is disabled under `prefers-reduced-motion`.
5. The owner can hide the card; the choice is kept in the same storage key.

## 20. Campaign results import (CSV)

Files: `src/lib/perf-import.ts`, `src/lib/perf-store.ts`, `src/app/api/performance/route.ts`, `src/components/app/PerformanceImport.tsx` (on the reports page).

1. The owner exports an ad-level report (Meta, Snapchat, TikTok, Google Ads, or an email tool) and drops the CSV on the reports page. The browser reads the text (2 MB at most) and posts `{csv, file, metric?}` to `POST /api/performance`.
2. `readRows`: RFC 4180 parse with the delimiter detected from the header; the header is the first of the first twelve rows naming two known columns (English or Arabic synonyms, most specific first: "link clicks" before "clicks"); totals lines are skipped; at most 5,000 rows. Names and text are stripped to one plain line (`clean`). Missing name column or metrics: 422 with a reason code.
3. `analyze`: the metric defaults to what the file supports (ROAS, then cost per result, CTR, open rate, conversion rate) and can be switched. A row is ranked only above the metric's sample minimum (500 impressions for CTR, 30 clicks for cost per result, 50 spend for ROAS, 30 clicks for conversion rate, 100 sends for open rate). Findings: top and bottom three, best against the median, wording patterns (offer, number, question, Arabic, short) compared by pooled metric when both groups have three or more ads and differ by 20% or more, best and weakest placement, and spend with no result when it is 10% or more of spend. Confidence is "medium" at ten times the sample minimum with five or more ads, otherwise "low"; never "high".
4. `savePerf` stores the snapshot in `perfimport:{scope}` and writes `performance-learnings.md`, which `memoryBlock` includes, so every later generation, the weekly plan included, reads it. The upload's own totals go to `adstats:{scope}:csv` in the same `Promise.all` (section 50). The file states that quoted names are data, not instructions, and that findings are comparisons, not promises. The reports card links to chat with prompts to write variants of the winners or plan next week.
5. No model call and no credits. `DELETE` removes the snapshot, the file and `adstats:{scope}:csv`. An upload replaces the last snapshot and the file, so replacing is deleting: `POST` needs admin+ (`performance.import`) and `DELETE` needs admin+ (`performance.delete`). A connected ad account's sync (section 21) reads the account's own numbers and stays open to every login. A member sees the findings with a note instead of the upload and remove controls.

## 21. Ad results from Meta and Snapchat (read only)

Files: `src/lib/connectors/ads.ts`, `src/lib/perf-store.ts` (`syncAds`, `syncAdsIfDue`), `api/connect/[provider]` (`sync_ads`, `select_ad_account`), `api/connect/[provider]/callback` (`connectAds`), `api/cron/daily`, `PerformanceImport.tsx`, `IntegrationsPanel.tsx`.

1. Connect: `meta_ads` is a separate OAuth connection from `meta` (Pages) with the scope `ads_read` only, so a pending App Review of `ads_read` never breaks Page publishing; it uses the same `META_APP_ID` and `META_APP_SECRET`. `snapchat` uses `snapchat-marketing-api`. After the code exchange, `connectAds` trades Meta's token for a long-lived one (about 60 days; no refresh token, so `fresh.ts` asks for a reconnect when it lapses) and lists ad accounts (`GET /me/adaccounts`, active only; Snap `GET /me/organizations?with_ad_accounts=true`, ACTIVE only). The first is chosen; `meta.adAccounts` lists the rest by id, name and currency. No active account: the callback returns `error=no_ad_account` and nothing is saved.
2. Read: `POST /api/connect/{meta_ads|snapchat} {action:"sync_ads"}`. Window: the last 30 whole UTC days ending yesterday. Meta: `GET /{act}/insights?level=ad&fields=ad_id,ad_name,impressions,inline_link_clicks,spend,actions,action_values&time_range=…`, following `paging.next` only on the Graph host (10 pages, 2,000 rows at most); purchases and purchase value from `omni_purchase`, else `purchase`, else the pixel purchase, never summed; ad copy from `GET /?ids=…&fields=creative{body,title}` for up to 50 ads, best effort. Snapchat: ad names from `GET /adaccounts/{id}/ads`, then `GET /adaccounts/{id}/stats?granularity=TOTAL&breakdown=ad&fields=impressions,swipes,spend,conversion_purchases,conversion_purchases_value`; spend and value are micro-currency and divided by 1,000,000; swipes are the clicks.
3. The rows go through `analyze` (section 20) and `savePerf` with `source` set to the provider, so the latest results, from an upload or an account, are what `performance-learnings.md` holds. `adsync:{scope}:{provider}` records the read time, and `adstats:{scope}:{provider}` keeps this account's own totals and, where one was read, the previous 30 days' (section 50, steps 1 and 6). No model call and no credits.
4. Daily: `dailyFor` calls `syncAdsIfDue`, which reads the connected ad account whose last read is oldest, once it is more than 20 hours old, one platform a day. A failure is recorded in the outcome (`ads_failed:…`) and does not stop the rest of the workspace's day; a 401 or 403 marks the connection for reconnecting.
5. Switch account: `{action:"select_ad_account", accountId}` accepts only an id in `meta.adAccounts`. Switching needs admin+ (`connectors`); `sync_ads` is any login's.
6. Nothing creates, edits, pauses or funds an ad. The readers send no request other than GET, apart from Meta's token exchange.
7. Through Composio (2026-10-05, branch `claude/composio-api-install-1d5bfc`, rules 653-655): when `COMPOSIO_PROVIDERS` names `meta_ads`, `snapchat`, `google_ads` or `tiktok_ads`, Connect goes through Composio (section 11a) with the toolkit `metaads`, `snapchat`, `googleads` or `tiktok_ads`. `connectViaComposio` lists the ad accounts with `adAccountsVia` (`src/lib/connectors/ads-via-composio.ts`: Meta `act_` accounts with status 1, Snapchat accounts of the login's organisations with a UUID id and status ACTIVE, Google customers from the accessible list, at most 10, manager accounts skipped, TikTok advertisers with an ENABLE status) and stores them with `accountMeta`; none answers `error=no_ad_account`. `syncAds` reads a Composio record with `adRowsVia`: Meta insights per ad paged by the cursor Meta returned; Snapchat ads (running first, at most 50) with stats per ad, money in micro-units; Google one constant GAQL query (`googleQuery`, only the two computed dates vary), `cost_micros / 1e6`; TikTok the integrated report per ad, revenue = spend × `complete_payment_roas`. One failed Snapchat or Google item is skipped; all failed throws the first error so a refused token asks for a reconnect. Google Ads and TikTok Ads have no direct path (`COMPOSIO_ONLY`). The catalog lists no write tool for these toolkits, so nothing here can create, edit, pause or fund an ad (the founder's rule of 2026-10-05: ads are fetched and analysed only). Meta's creative text is not read: wording findings use ad names only.

## 21a. Site figures from Google Analytics 4 and Search Console (read only, 2026-10-05, rule 656)

Files: `src/lib/site-stats.ts`, `src/lib/connectors/via-composio.ts` (`gaProperties`, `gscSites`), `api/connect/[provider]/callback`, `api/cron/daily`, `src/lib/stats.ts`, `src/components/app/SiteStats.tsx`, the Reports page.

1. Connect: the `google` provider exists only through Composio (`COMPOSIO_ONLY`), with two toolkits on one record: `google_analytics` (the first `properties/{digits}` the login can read; none answers `no_ga_property`) and `google_search_console` (the first verified site, `sc-domain:` or an http(s) URL; none answers `no_gsc_site`). Other properties and sites are kept in `meta.gaProperties` and `meta.gscSites`, and the owner or an admin chooses among them with `select_property` (section 50 step 8). A first read runs in the callback; its failure is logged and the daily run retries.
2. Read (`syncSite`): GA4 sessions, users, engagement rate, key events and purchase revenue by default channel group plus the top 10 landing pages, over the 28 days ending yesterday; Search Console clicks, impressions and position by date (position weighted by impressions) plus the top 15 queries, over the 28 days ending three days before the read. Both parts run together; one failing keeps the other and records a short error code. Both failing throws the first error. Stored at `sitestats:{scope}`. Each part also reads the 28 days before its window, and a figure not read is null (section 50 step 7).
3. Daily: after `syncAdsIfDue`, `dailyFor` calls `syncSiteIfDue`, which reads a Composio Google record whose last read is more than 20 hours old; a refused token marks the record for reconnect. `syncSiteIfDue(scope, now, true)` reads at once whatever the age, and `readSiteSoon` runs it after the response (section 50 step 7); any login's Refresh (`sync_site`) runs it at once, six an hour per workspace (section 50 step 9).
4. Dana: `statsFor` adds `siteDataBlock` after the store block: the totals inside `<site_data>` serialised with `asData`, queries and page paths cleaned and labelled as data from the public, with rules (null is not read; compare only with a part's `prev`, no change over time without one, section 50 step 7; the revenue's currency is not named so it is never converted or added to store or ad figures; users are never summed).
5. Reports: the site card (`SiteStats.tsx`) shows the same totals, a dash for a figure not read and a change only where `prev` was read; a direct (non-Composio) `google` record does not count as an analytics source.
6. Nothing writes to Google: the catalog lists no write tool for either toolkit.

## 21b. Instagram and LinkedIn page results (read only, 2026-10-08, rules 663-673)

Files: `src/lib/social-stats.ts`, `src/lib/publish-once.ts`, `api/cron/daily`, `src/lib/stats.ts`. Spec: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-08-social-results-via-composio-design.md`.

1. Parts: Instagram is read when the `meta` record is a Composio one holding an Instagram account and `meta.igId`; the LinkedIn page when the `linkedin` record is a Composio one with `meta.liOrg`; the Facebook Page when the `meta` record is a Composio one with the `facebook` part and a digits-only `meta.pageId` (section 50 step 13). A part not held is skipped, not failed. LinkedIn: `LINKEDIN_GET_SHARE_STATS` for the page over the last 28 whole days and the 28 before them (56 days in one call, split by day: section 50 step 11; DAY rows, summed in code: impressions, clicks, likes as reactions, comments, reposts; engagement is their sum over impressions, `null` at zero) and `LINKEDIN_GET_NETWORK_SIZE` (`COMPANY_FOLLOWED_BY_MEMBER`) for followers, `null` when that read fails, and the page's own views from `LINKEDIN_GET_ORG_PAGE_STATS` (section 50 step 12), `null` when that read fails. A 403 on the share statistics is `li_no_page_access`, written and not thrown.
2. Read (`syncSocial`): Instagram username and followers, account reach and views over 28 days and over the 28 days before (`prev`, section 50 step 11), then the posts of the last 28 days (25 per page, following Instagram's cursor until 30 posts or a repeated cursor; `paging.next` is never stored), then each post's views, reach, likes, comments, saves and shares four at a time, with one retry without views and shares. A timeout or a rate limit is not retried, and past the shared 30-second deadline (section 50 step 11) no further post (or media page) is asked for: those posts keep `null` figures. A number not read is `null`, never zero. Captions are cleaned and cut to 80 characters; a post link is kept only on `instagram.com`. A post is marked as Thakeen's from `socialpub:{scope}`, the post ids `publishOnce` recorded. Both parts run together and the snapshot at `socialstats:{scope}` keeps what succeeded, with a short error code per failed part (the v2 shape and its monthly history: section 50 step 10). A refused token is thrown with its provider after the rest is written; a personal Instagram account is recorded as `ig_not_professional` and not thrown, but only when the platform's refusal says so (professional, business account, nonexisting field, unsupported get request): a network failure, a timeout or an unknown error is a failed read and the last good snapshot stays; anything else is thrown only when nothing was read.
3. Daily: last in `dailyFor`, after the brief and its email, so a slow platform never costs the workspace its brief, `dailyFor` calls `syncSocialIfDue`, which reads the held parts whose records are not waiting for a reconnect, once the last read is more than 20 hours old. A failure is reported as `social_failed:…` and the workspace's day goes on; a refused token marks that record for reconnect. Any login's Refresh (`sync_social` on `meta`, `linkedin`, `youtube` or `tiktok`) runs `syncSocialIfDue(scope, now, true)` at once, six an hour per workspace (section 50 step 9).
4. Dana: `statsFor` adds `socialDataBlock` after the site block: the account totals and posts inside `<social_data>` serialised with `asData`, labelled as data, with rules (compare only with `prev`, never invent a change, section 50 step 10; followers are a count on the read date; each post's figures are its lifetime totals on the read date; LinkedIn engagement is a fraction, 0.0733 meaning 7.33%; `null` is unknown, never zero; a read older than 7 days carries its date).
5. Reports: the social card (`SocialStats.tsx`) shows the account, its followers, 28-day reach and views, and the five best posts by views then reach, a dash for a number not read, a link only to the platform's own post page (Instagram, or https `facebook.com` with no query string for the Facebook Page; `noopener noreferrer`), and «من ذكين» on Thakeen's posts; a personal account shows why it has no results. The LinkedIn page shows its followers, 28-day impressions, engagement rate and actions; a Composio LinkedIn record with no page chosen (`liNeedsPage`) or a 403 shows «اربط صفحة شركة». The followers tile unlocks only once a part was read (`socialRead`); a record counts as a social source only once a part can be read (`connectedSources` in `report-sources.ts`): a Composio Meta record with `meta.igId` or `meta.pageId` (the Facebook Page, read since rule 739), a Composio LinkedIn record with `meta.liOrg`; a direct record or a LinkedIn record with no page keeps the banner.
6. Nothing posts, replies or edits: the catalog lists the read tools only (rule 663).
7. LinkedIn pages (rule 671): connecting LinkedIn through Composio also reads `LINKEDIN_GET_COMPANY_INFO` (approved roles only) and keeps up to 20 company pages the member administers (`ADMINISTRATOR` or `CONTENT_ADMINISTRATOR`, URN `urn:li:organization:{digits}`) in `meta.liOrgs`, with cleaned names. One page is chosen at once (`meta.liOrg`); with several, the page chosen before stays only if still listed. A refused page list leaves the connect as it was, with no pages. Every connect drops `liPostAs`. `POST /api/connect/linkedin {action:"select_org", org}` (admin+) accepts only a listed URN (400 `unknown_org`) and sets posting back to the member, writing a `connector_changed` row `linkedin:post_as:me` when the page had been posting; it then reads the new page's results at once. The Integrations card shows the page picker.
8. Post as the page (rule 672): `POST /api/connect/linkedin {action:"post_as", value}` (admin+) takes only `me` or `org` (400 `bad_value`) and `org` only with a chosen page (400 `no_page`). `postToLinkedInVia` posts as `meta.liOrg` only when `liPostAs` is `org`; absent means the member. Choosing another page (`select_org`) and every reconnect set posting back to the member. The Integrations card shows «انشر باسم» only once a page is chosen.
9. First read: after a Composio connect of Instagram (the `meta` record, `instagram` toolkit) or LinkedIn, and after `select_org`, `readSocialSoon` reads at once whatever the last read's age, after the response (`after`); a failure only logs `social first read failed` and the daily run reads again. A LinkedIn connect with several pages and none chosen reads nothing until a page is chosen. Amended 2026-10-08 (section 50 steps 13-15): every Composio social connect (the Facebook Page, a YouTube channel, a TikTok account too) reads at once, and so does `select_page` on a Composio Meta record while the workspace's `socialsync` bucket lasts.

## 22. Ad codes: measuring Nashr's own ads

Files: `src/lib/ad-codes.ts`, `saveTask` in `src/lib/brand.ts`, `analyze` in `src/lib/perf-import.ts`, `Inbox.tsx`, `PerformanceImport.tsx`.

1. `saveTask` gives every new `ads` and `social` item an `adCode` (`NSR-` plus five characters from an alphabet without 0, O, 1, I and L) and writes `adcodes:{scope}` code → task id in the same round of commands. A later save keeps the code.
2. The inbox card shows the code with a copy button and the hint to put it in the ad's name in the ads manager. Nothing writes names on the platforms: the connectors are read only.
3. When results come in (CSV upload, section 20, or an ad account, section 21), `analyze` receives the workspace's code index. A row whose name or copy contains one of those codes is a Nashr ad; any other code is ignored. `snapshot.nashr` lists them with their metric and sample, and compares the pooled metric of the ranked Nashr ads with the other ranked ads when there are at least 2 and 3, with a confidence of low or medium by the same rule as the other findings. Codes are removed from the text before the wording patterns run.
4. `performance-learnings.md` gains a "Thakeen's own ads" section with the comparison, telling Noor to build on what worked and to say plainly when Thakeen's ads did worse. The results card shows the section, or how to add codes when none are found.

## 23. Media generation (images and video)

Files: `src/lib/media-catalog.ts`, `src/lib/media/{types,keys,config,fal,kie,higgsfield,blob,prompts,jobs,registry,start}.ts`, `src/app/api/tasks/[id]/{image,video}/route.ts`, `src/app/api/media/[jobId]/route.ts`, `src/app/api/media/hook/[provider]/route.ts`, `src/app/api/cron/media/route.ts`, `src/components/app/{Inbox,MediaControls}.tsx`, `src/app/[lang]/app/page.tsx`, chat tool `generate_image` (`src/lib/chat.ts:815`). Design: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-29-media-providers-design.md`.

1. Catalogue (`MEDIA_MODELS`). Eleven models, each with a label, a hint and a credit price shown on the choice, and the price shown is the price charged. Images: `img-fast` (fal, 1), `img-balanced` (kie, 1), `img-premium` (kie, 2), `img-text` (kie, 1, English headline only), `img-lifestyle` (Higgsfield, 1), `img-design` (Higgsfield, 1). Video: `vid-quick` (kie, 12, 5 s), `vid-sound` (kie, 12, 8 s), `vid-premium` (kie, 24, 10 s), `vid-cinematic` (Higgsfield, 12, 5 s), `vid-motion` (Higgsfield, 12, 6 s, needs the post's image). Images for `IMAGE_TYPES` (social, ads, gbp, whatsapp, cart_recovery), video for `VIDEO_TYPES` (social, ads), both checked on the server. Aspect: 9:16 for video and for the TikTok and Snapchat channels, 1:1 otherwise (`aspectFor`).
2. What is offered (`modelAvailable` in `src/lib/media/config.ts`): the provider's keys (`FAL_KEY`; `KIE_API_KEY`; both Higgsfield keys); the Blob store for kie and Higgsfield, whose results are deleted after 1 to 14 days while posts are published by URL later (fal works without it and hot-links); the model listed in `MEDIA_MODELS_ON` (`img-fast` is always on; unset means `img-fast` only); and not switched off in `media:off`. The inbox page reads `availableModels(undefined, await offModels())` per request and passes it to the inbox with the balance, the plan and `paused`; the start routes check the same things again. When the default image model is unavailable, the image route and the chat tool fall back to the placeholder card, uncharged, and only onto an item without artwork: the card is written through `updateTask` on the task as stored, and an item that already has a generated image (or gets one from another image style while the placeholder waits for the task lock) keeps it, with 409 `model_unavailable` and nothing written (`placeholder`, `src/lib/media/start.ts:47-70`; section 8 step 12); an image of another style that finishes after the card was written replaces the card, because the card leaves `task.mediaJob` as it was. The inbox follows the same rule (`MediaControls.tsx`): with no image model offered, an item with artwork keeps its image button closed with the `keepsImage` note, and a 409 `model_unavailable` to a request sent without a model shows that note too.
3. Start (spec 4.4 A): `architecture.md` section 4(e) steps 1-4, and section 8 step 12 here for the credits. Role: any login; the image and video routes and the poll check the session only, with no role action (`tests/role-gates.test.ts` lists `POST /api/tasks/[id]/image`, `POST /api/tasks/[id]/video` and `GET /api/media/[jobId]` as `member`), and the render is charged to the account (section 26 step 16). Deadline: `deadlineFor(maxDuration)` = start + `maxDuration` × 1000 − 15 s. Each step takes `min(its maximum, time left)`: prompt 20 s; submit 45 s for fal (keeping 25 s for the copy) and 20 s for kie and Higgsfield; status 15 s; download 15 s (image) or 45 s (video); put 20 s or 30 s. No step starts below its minimum (a submit with less than 5 s left is not sent, and the credit comes back). The chat tool works to the chat request's own deadline: `deadlineFor(300)` counted from the start of `chat()` (`CHAT_MAX_DURATION_S`, the `maxDuration` of `/api/chat` and `/api/mcp`, pinned by a test), or `opts.deadline` when the route passes one; with less than 60 s of it left (`CHAT_RENDER_MIN_MS`) it starts no render and charges nothing.
4. Reference image (spec A9, A26, `mediaImageFor`): only our Blob copy of a real generated image (`hasArtwork` and `isOurBlobUrl`) is ever sent to a provider, and only to the models that build on it. Video takes it as a first frame only when it is portrait (TikTok and Snapchat posts); with a square image the clip is text-to-video at 9:16, except Photo to motion, which needs the image whatever its shape (the inbox says the clip keeps the photo's square shape, `squareClip`).
5. Prompts (spec 4.5, `src/lib/media/prompts.ts`). With Claude on: one Haiku call (`claude-haiku-5-5` since 2026-10-09, `claude-sonnet-5` before, `max_tokens` 400, effort low, `{timeout: min(20 s, time left), maxRetries: 0}`), metered as `image_prompt` for images and video alike; the text style asks for one English headline of at most 6 words and no Arabic lettering, and video prompts forbid speech, dialogue and voice-over. On a failure, an empty reply, less than 3 s left, or while paused: the fixed template (section 7 step 11). The `img-text` template quotes the brand name only when it is plain Latin text, else it is the design template; the task title never reaches it. The prompt is shown under the image or the clip.
6. Poll (spec 4.4 E). `pollJob` in `MediaControls.tsx` asks `GET /api/media/{jobId}` every 3 s (image) or 6 s (video) for the first 2 minutes, then every 15 s; a failed request (a 429, any other error answer, or a network error) doubles the gap up to 30 s. Past the model's max age plus 2 minutes (`GRACE`) the card shows the `stillRendering` note once and keeps asking once a minute (`LATE_GAP`) until the server settles the job: only `done`, `failed`, a 404 or a 401 ends the loop. The running job is found through `task.mediaJob[kind]` as well as the click, so a reload resumes polling; after a 409 `in_progress` that named no job, the card re-reads the task at 4, 12 and 30 s (`RECHECK_GAPS`) and then stops. The route answers `{ok, job}` plus `task` when done and the account's `credits` when failed; `publicJob` carries no email, scope or prompt, and `queued` reads as `running`.
7. `advance` (`jobs.ts`): a terminal job returns as it is. A `queued` job belongs to its start request for 3 minutes (longer than any route deadline); after that, with the job lock free, it fails `submit_lost` and is refunded. Otherwise a status call needs the `mpoll:{jobId}` throttle (4 s; skipped only by the sweeps' `force`) and the job lock `mjoblock:{jobId}` (120 s), held through finalize or fail, so two tabs, webhooks and sweeps cannot multiply provider calls and a job being copied gets none. A job holding `resultUrls` is copied without a status call. `pending` past the max age (image 10 minutes, video 30) is cancelled (Higgsfield, best effort) and fails `timeout`; `done` is finalized when the deadline leaves room (25 s for an image, 60 s for a clip), else kept for the next poll; `failed` ends `nsfw` or `provider_failed`. Every fail goes through `failLocked`, which refunds through `refundOnce` (section 8 step 12) before it writes the task and then the job; while another pass holds the refund claim it throws `refund_pending` and the job stays open.
8. Finalize and storage (spec 4.6, A25). `copyToBlob` downloads the result through `safeFetch`, caps it (image 25 MB, video 100 MB), checks the magic bytes, re-encodes images with `sharp` (rotated upright, transparency on white, fitted inside 1440×1920, JPEG quality 88) and stores MP4 unchanged under `media/{32 hex}`. A failed copy keeps the result on the job (`resultUrls`) and is retried by later polls or sweeps without another status call, at least 30 s apart, 3 tries in all; then `store_failed`, refunded, with the provider's cost recorded. The engine checks the 30 s itself (`copyWaits`): the `mpoll` lock holds polls back, and a sweep's `force`, which skips that lock, still waits. On delivery the task is written first (`updateTask`, `jobs.ts:470`) and only then the job is saved `done`; a failed task write leaves the job `running` with its `resultUrls`, so the next poll or sweep copies and delivers it instead of a done job the task never received. `withResult` (`jobs.ts:413`) adds the job's credits to `mediaCredits` and appends the job id to `mediaApplied` (the last 8, `APPLIED_KEEP`) in the same write, and returns null (nothing saved) for a job already in that list, so a retry after a write whose index half was lost never counts the render twice. fal keeps its own URL when the copy fails (the hot-link used before Blob). The task gets the media fields only while `task.mediaJob[kind]` still points at this job, so a late, older job never overwrites a newer render.
9. Webhook (spec 4.4 D, A30): `architecture.md` section 4(e) step 6. Sweeps (spec 4.4 F, A3): `sweepMedia` reads the three open sets with one `Promise.all`, loads the jobs with `pmap` (8 at a time), removes members that are gone, terminal or unparseable, and force-advances jobs older than 2 minutes or holding `resultUrls`, oldest first, 4 at a time, starting none with less than 20 s left (section 16 for the two callers).
10. Higgsfield admission (spec A10, `hfAdmission`): every tenant shares one Higgsfield account, so only live jobs count (not terminal, inside their max age, no result waiting to be copied). At the cap the start first force-advances stale open jobs (at most 4, behind `mhfclean`, 60 s, status calls only). Then at most 1 live Higgsfield job per account (429 `one_open`) and 2 in all (`HF_MAX_OPEN`), of which at most 1 a video (429 `busy`). The check is not atomic; Higgsfield's own concurrency refusal is `busy` too, refunded at once.
11. Breaker (spec A22, `trip` and `setOff`): 3 `store_failed`, `timeout` or defect-class `submit_failed` (`rejected`, `bad_response`) of one model within an hour switch it off for 6 hours; a kie render whose `creditsConsumed` exceeds 110 % of `EXPECTED_KIE_CREDITS` switches its model off for 6 hours (the owner keeps that render); a `no_balance` answer switches every model of that provider off for 1 hour. A switched-off model is refused (409 `model_unavailable`, or the placeholder for `img-fast`) before any charge and left out of the inbox. Entries clear themselves at `until`, or when `media:off` is deleted.
12. Notifications (spec A28): a job that ends through a webhook or a sweep (`via` `hook` or `sweep`) tells the owner with `pushNotif` (kind `info`), in the language stored on the job: ready, or did not finish with the credits returned. Only the account that was charged, and only while it exists with the same `createdAt`. A failure also leaves `task.mediaLast[kind] = {error, credits, refunded, at}` on the card until the next render of that kind starts.
13. Chat tool `generate_image`: the default model only (spec A2), through `renderImage`, with the deadline of step 3. Its answers name the outcome: not started because the reply has run too long (no credit used), a placeholder with no credit used ("not available right now", since the breaker may have switched the default model off), no image model while the item already has artwork ("keeps its current image. No credit was used"), trial ended, insufficient credits, wrong item type, already being drawn, busy ("try again in a minute. No credit was used."), the account limit (30 an hour, 150 a day), a content refusal or any other failure, saying the credit was returned only when the engine's answer carries the refunded balance (otherwise that a credit taken is returned on its own within a few minutes), still being drawn ("will appear in the inbox shortly"), or generated with the balance. `list_inbox` marks items with `(has image)` and `(has video)`. MCP has no media tool of its own, but its `ask_noor` runs this chat loop, so it reaches `generate_image` under the same rules and deadline.
14. Video in the inbox: a preview with a poster frame (`#t=0.1`), save or share (`navigator.share` with files, else a `?download=1` link on our Blob host), and `videoNoPublish` under every clip.
15. Retention. Blob files and images hot-linked at fal.ai survive account deletion: `wipeAccount` does not touch them (a follow-up in the spec, section 9). The job records (`mjob:`, 7 days from the last save), the webhook pointers (`mjobref:`, 7 days), the trial marker (`mtrialvid:`, 14 days), the flight locks and the per-account render counters expire by TTL, so the render records of a deleted account are gone within 14 days. The privacy policy and the help centre say this.

## 24. Updates and wishlist (`/{lang}/updates`, 2026-09-29)

Files: `src/app/[lang]/updates/page.tsx`, `src/lib/changelog.ts` (content), `src/lib/wishlist.ts` (storage), `src/components/WishVote.tsx`, `src/components/WishSuggest.tsx`, `src/app/api/wishlist/vote/route.ts`, `src/app/api/wishlist/suggest/route.ts`, `src/app/api/admin/wishlist/route.ts`, `src/components/app/WishlistPanel.tsx`.

1. Linked from the marketing nav and footer (the Resources cards in `site.ts`), the sitemap, and the app sidebar (`N.updates`).
2. The page is dynamic. It reads `currentUser()` (optional) and `listWishes(email)`: the hidden set, the viewer's `wish:mine:{email}`, `wish:items` and `wish:status` side by side, then every item's count in one pipelined `scardMany`. Counts and the viewer's pressed state are in the server HTML; the two sections ("What's new", "Wishlist") are anchors, so nothing needs JavaScript to read.
3. "What's new" is `CHANGELOG` in `changelog.ts`: dated entries (merge date on main), tag new / improved / fixed, bilingual title and one line. Edited in code, with the change it describes.
4. The wishlist is `SEED_WISHES` (code; from the «أمنية: نعم» rows of `docs/Competitor_Features_AR.md`, no competitor names, no vote numbers) plus items the founder promoted (`wish:items`), less seeded items the founder hid (`wish:hidden`), with status from `wish:status` (default planned). Order: in progress, planned, shipped; most votes first; ties keep the seed order.
5. Vote: the button posts `{id, on}` to `/api/wishlist/vote`. Session first (401 sends the browser to login with `next=/{lang}/updates`), 60 per hour per account, id must be votable, then `sadd`/`srem` on `wish:votes:{id}` and `wish:mine:{email}` side by side, and the new count. `on` states the wanted result, so repeating a request is harmless; without `on` it toggles. The button updates at the click and restores its state if the request fails. Any role may vote; the vote counts for the account (`u.email` is the owner's).
6. Suggest: signed-in users post `{text, lang}` to `/api/wishlist/suggest`; cleaned, 5..280 characters, 5 per day; stored in `wish:suggestions` (capped at 500). Never shown publicly. Any role may suggest; the suggestion is filed under the account's email.
7. Founder console, Wishlist tab (`WishlistPanel`): reads `GET /api/admin/wishlist`; promotes a suggestion under a bilingual title the founder writes (the matching-language field is pre-filled with the suggestion as a draft), adds a wish directly, sets statuses, hides / restores seeded items, deletes promoted items with their votes (after a yes in the app's confirmation dialog since 2026-10-08, rule 695; hiding a seeded item asks nothing), dismisses suggestions. Each action goes to `admin:log` as `wish:*`. Founder console only (`isAdmin`).
8. Account deletion (`wipeAccount`): `forgetWishlistUser` removes the account from every item it voted for, removes its suggestions, and deletes `wish:mine:{email}`.

## 25. Reem, the secretary (phase 1, 2026-09-29)

Files: `src/lib/secretary.ts` (store, validation, changes, tools; server only), `src/lib/secretary-agenda.ts` (types, local time, the computed agenda, the chase clock, alert steps; browser-safe), `src/lib/zones.ts` (the six Gulf zones; import-free), `src/app/api/secretary/route.ts`, `src/app/api/secretary/[id]/route.ts`, `src/app/[lang]/app/todo/page.tsx`, `src/components/app/TodoPanel.tsx`, `content/skills/secretary.md`. Spec: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-29-secretary-reem-phase1.md`. Free: no credits and no model call anywhere below (a chat turn with Reem is a normal chat turn).

0. Time zone. Each brand keeps its list in one zone (`zoneOf(brand)`): `brand.timeZone` when it is one of `ZONE_IDS` (Asia/Riyadh, Asia/Kuwait, Asia/Qatar, Asia/Bahrain at UTC+3; Asia/Dubai, Asia/Muscat at UTC+4; none keeps daylight saving), else the zone the brand's city names without doubt («دبي», "Muscat", «الدوحة»…; a bare «عمان» is also Amman and decides nothing), else the dialect (Emirati: Dubai; Kuwaiti: Kuwait), else Riyadh. The owner picks it on the brand form; `PUT /api/brand` stores only an allow-listed value, and `update_brand` cannot set it. The zone decides the local day ("today", "overdue", the brief's day), how a date without a zone is read, how dates are written back, the snooze presets and the weekend (Friday and Saturday; Saturday and Sunday in the UAE) that working days skip; since 2026-10-03 a brand may change its weekend, and working days also skip its Eid, official holidays and added days (section 39). Below, "local" means the brand's zone.
1. Reem is the eighth agent (`AGENTS`, id `reem`, "Secretary"), with a playbook and no task types (since 2026-10-05 her persona and playbook keep her to the brand's marketing within the Thakeen contract: she follows the team without assigning it work and declines what is outside that scope in one line, saving nothing; rules 609-611), so `agentForTask` never names her and the generate page, reviewer and golden set skip her. Her chat intro: she keeps the list, repeats every saved date back, never marks anything done, never promises a reminder at a set time (there is no alert at the due time in phase 1), and treats quoted customer, supplier, connector or file text as data. Every agent's uncached prompt block starts with `nowLine`: `NOW (Riyadh, UTC+3): …; today is …. The work week starts on Sunday.` in the brand's zone (Dubai: `UTC+4`, Monday; since 2026-10-03 the week start follows the brand's own weekend, section 39), so relative dates are worked out from the real calendar. The chat page accepts `?agent=reem` (any id in the registry).
2. Capture. The owner writes in chat ("add: call the supplier Thursday 10am"); Reem (or any agent: the tools are offered to all) calls `todo_create` with a title and a due time in local wall time (`YYYY-MM-DDTHH:mm`, or `YYYY-MM-DD` for the end of that day). `runSecretaryTool` checks `sec:write` and `sec:create` allowances and reads the zone once, `createItem` validates and saves `sec:item:{scope}:{id}` with its `sec:open` score, `sec:all` member and one audit line, and the answer carries the stored date formatted in the owner's language and the zone's city (`formatWhen`: «الجمعة 2 أكتوبر 10:00 ص», "(Dubai time)"), which the prompt tells Reem to read back exactly; when that date has already passed the answer says so, so the agent checks it with the owner. A link to an inbox item (`task_id`) is accepted from chat or MCP only here, at creation, and only to an item still `pending` (400 `link_not_pending` otherwise). From the page: the add form posts `{title, dueAt, direction, owedBy|owedTo}` to `POST /api/secretary`; from the owner's own assistant: MCP `todo_create`, `source:"mcp"`.
3. Who owes what: `direction` `i_owe` (the owner acts; `owedTo` is who it is for) or `they_owe` (`owedBy` names who owes the owner; required; `owedTo` becomes `owner`). A `waiting_on` item is always `they_owe`. A party is `owner`, an exact agent id, or a contact label; Arabic names stay labels. A they-owe item without a due date gets `expectBy`: the owner's date (`expect_by` in the tools, `expectBy` on the route) or, by default, two working days after creation (`addWorkingDays`, skipping the zone's weekend, and since 2026-10-03 every quiet day of the brand's work calendar, section 39); an item that becomes they-owe or loses its date later gets it from then.
4. The agenda (`agendaFor`, `computeAgenda`), computed at every read and never stored: a proposed "done" goes to "needs your confirmation", even if the item was snoozed; other snoozed items are only counted until `snoozeUntil`. Someone else's item follows the chase clock (`chaseAt`): its due time, else `expectBy` (default as above), and after a recorded follow-up two working days after `lastChasedAt`, never before the due time (since 2026-10-03 through the one chase rule `chaseMoment`, which also waits out quiet days, Ramadan's off-hours and a contact's absence, section 39 step 5). Past the clock it is the owner's move: "overdue" (next step chase) when the due time passed, else "time to follow up" (`chase`); before it, "waiting on others" (reason `chased` when a follow-up holds an overdue item back). The owner's own items: past due goes to "overdue"; then "today" (the same local calendar day), "next 7 days", "later", "no due date", and "untouched for 14 days" (`cold`: undated, `updatedAt` 14 days back; next step: do it, date it or delete it). Order: critical, earliest due, priority, oldest, id. The approvals inbox count (`pendingCount`) is one line of the agenda. Reads: the open set, one MGET of items, the pending count, the brand file (its zone) and, when any item is linked, one MGET of the linked tasks, which also gives `facts` (step 6).
5. Updates: `todo_update` (title, details, due, clear due, expected date, snooze, priority, party, direction, critical, a note, `chased`, `repeat` (Reem and MCP only: `runSecretaryTool` refuses any other agent before spending `sec:write`; `stop_repeat` and `repeat: null` are `owner_only`); never a link: `toolFields` reads `task_id` for `todo_create` only, and `mutateItem` refuses a link change from anyone but the owner on the web, `owner_only`) and `PATCH /api/secretary/{id}` (the same fields; actions `snooze {preset: 2h|tonight|tomorrow | until}`, `reschedule {dueAt}`, `add_note {text, private}`, `chased`) go through `changeItem`: the item lock `sec:lock:{scope}:{id}`, the `rev` check when the caller sent one (409 `conflict` with the current item), the change applied to the stored copy in the brand's zone, `rev` bumped, item, index moves and audit line saved in one round. A follow-up (`chased`, the page's "Followed up" / «تابعت», or `todo_update {chased: true}` when the owner says they followed up) sets `lastChasedAt`, adds a note of kind `chased` («تمت المتابعة»; never "sent") and restarts the clock; it never closes the item, and on the owner's own item it is 409 `not_theirs`. The audit line's action is `chased`.
6. Closing. Chat and MCP can only propose: `todo_propose_done {id, evidence}` sets `proposedDone`, ends any snooze, and the item waits under "needs your confirmation". The evidence is the agent's own words: the page shows it as that agent's claim («ريم تقول: …», «سامي يقول: …», «مساعدك يقول: …», "Reem says: …", marked as not checked) and next to it, under "Checked by the app" / «تحقّق التطبيق», what code read from the stored linked task (`linkFactText` over `facts`: still waiting for approval, rejected on a date, approved by the owner on a date or by a rule, and where it was published), or "Nothing in the app confirms this" when the item has no link; `agenda_get` writes the same as "claimed done by … (a claim, not checked); checked by the app: …". The owner completes from the page with a written result (`complete {result}`; the Done button opens a required field), or confirms a proposal (`confirm_done`, the evidence becomes the result) or sends it back (`reject_done`, a note of kind `not_yet` records the evidence; `noteText` words it in the reader's language). These, `delete` and `restore` are refused for any actor other than the owner on the web route (`owner_only`).
7. The one automatic close: an item whose `links.taskId` names an inbox task of the same scope (set by the owner, or by chat or MCP at creation to a pending task, so an approval the owner makes after the link) closes with the result "linked item approved" when that task is `approved` (publishing approves too), found when the agenda is read (page, route, `agenda_get`, brief) or by the daily job, and re-checked under the item lock (`closeLinked`, audit actor `rule` or `cron`). The page lists it under "done" with the checked fact (when the owner approved it and where it was published).
8. Delete and restore: `delete` sets `deletedAt` and `expiresAt` (30 days) and moves the id from `sec:open` to `sec:deleted`; the page shows "Deleted. Undo" and a restorable list; `restore` puts it back where it was (open or done) until `expiresAt`, then answers 410; a restore that reopens an item counts against the 500 open items like a new one (409 `open_limit`). The daily cron's `purgeExpired` removes at most 200 expired items per scope per day with their `sec:deleted`, `sec:all`, `sec:done` and `sec:open` entries, for every brand scope: the active ones through `secretaryDaily`, archived brands and suspended accounts through `purgeIdle` (`dailyRoster().idle`).
9. Brief: `runDaily` adds «مهامك (ريم): N متأخرة، N لليوم، N بانتظار غيرك» (and the numbers to follow up and to confirm; since 2026-10-03 on a Ramadan day a contact's chases whose window opens later today, with the time, section 39 step 6) with the first three overdue or due-today titles, stored as `Brief.agenda`; the brief email carries it, and the "important" preference sends when a to-do is overdue or due today.
10. Overdue alerts (`followUps` → `overdueTodoAlerts` in `src/lib/notifications.ts`): one `todo` notification from Reem per overdue item, linking to `/{lang}/app/todo`, on the local day it fell behind and on the 1st, 3rd and 7th day after (`overdueAlerts`, `alertStep`: day 0 → step 0, days 1-2 → 1, 3-6 → 3, day 7 → 7, later → none; a missed day inside a window is caught up once). Each step is claimed once with SET NX on `notifseen:{email}:todo-due:{scope}:{id}:{dueAt ms}:{step}` (7 days, set again by every daily run while its step holds), so a new due date starts afresh and two overlapping runs send it once. At most `SEC_ALERTS_PER_DAY` (5) a brand a local day, critical and earliest first, counted in `sec:alerts:{scope}:{day}` (2 days); a claim beyond the allowance is handed back (its marker deleted) so the item can still go on another day of its window. The seventh day's alert says it is the last; after it the item is only in the morning brief. Every other follow-up keeps its own fingerprint, now claimed the same atomic way (`pushNotif`). Since 2026-10-03 this holds as written for the owner's own items only; what someone else owes follows the chase rule, counted in working days for a contact and never sent on a quiet day, with the marker `todo-due:{scope}:{id}:{anchor ms}:{step}` (section 39 step 7).
11. The page `/{lang}/app/todo` ("مهامي" / "My to-dos", in the sidebar under Approvals) is rendered on the server from `todoPage(sc(u))`: sections per bucket, each row with its title, due date formatted in the brand's zone, who owes it, when it is expected and the last follow-up, badges, its last three notes (private ones included: this is the owner's page) and the next step, and Done (result required), Snooze (2 hours, tonight 8 PM while it is still ahead, tomorrow 9 AM, local), Reschedule (a datetime-local input read as local time), "Followed up" on someone else's item, and Delete (with Undo, a toast pinned to the bottom of the screen for 8 seconds; restoring from the Deleted list clears it); "needs your confirmation" rows show the claim and the checked fact and get Confirm / Not yet; then the add form, the last 10 done (with the checked fact of a linked item) and the restorable deleted items. The subtitle and the date labels name the zone («بتوقيت دبي»). Every action is one request followed by a refresh of the server render. There is no alert at the due time, and reminders on WhatsApp or Telegram are not available; the page says both.
12. Account deletion: `wipeAccount` deletes every key `secretaryKeys(scope)` names for every brand scope: each id in `sec:all`, the open, done, deleted and id sets, the audit log and the alert counters of the last days (since 2026-10-03 also `sec:cal`, `sec:away` and Reem's away proposals, section 39 step 11). The `notifseen:` alert markers expire within seven days: a daily run sets a step's marker again while its step holds, and no run reaches a deleted account.

## 26. Teams: members, invites and seats (2026-09-29)

Files: `src/lib/team.ts`, `src/lib/roles.ts`, `src/lib/auth.ts` (`resolveSession`, `getLogin`, `PLAN_SEATS`, `seatLimit`), `src/app/api/team/*`, `src/app/[lang]/app/team/page.tsx` (`TeamPanel`, `teamPerms`), `src/app/[lang]/app/join/page.tsx` (`JoinForm`, `inviteToken.ts`), `src/app/api/admin/plan/route.ts`, `src/lib/account.ts`. Design: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-29-teams-design.md`. Roles and gates: `permissions.md` 0.1.

1. Model. The account stays the owner's `User` at `user:{owner}`: plan, credits, brands, scopes, governance, usage and cron are unchanged. A member is a separate login at `member:{email}` (`{email, name, lang, passHash, memberOf, role, invitedBy, acceptedAt, sessionsAfter, disabled?}`), listed in `members:{owner}`. It never passes through `saveUser` or `createUser`, never enters `users:all`, and gets no trial credits, referral code or signup tracking. An email is one login across `user:` and `member:`: signup and accept both create logins under `lock:login:{email}`.
2. Members page (`/{lang}/app/team`, every role; "Members" / "الأعضاء"). Server-rendered from `teamView(u)`, the same body `GET /api/team` returns: the acting person, seats used of the limit, the roster (owner first; each member's role, `acceptedAt` and `disabled`) and, for admin and owner, the pending invites. The controls come from `teamPerms(u)`, which is `can()`; after every change the panel reloads `GET /api/team`.
3. Invite (`POST /api/team/invite {email, role, lang?}`). Needs `team.invite.member` (admin+); `role: "admin"` needs the owner; a founder impersonating cannot invite. Rate limits `team:invite:acct:{owner}` 20 a day (5 on a trial, which anyone can open with one signup), `team:invite:ip:{ip}` 30 an hour and `team:invite:to:{sha256(address)}` 5 a day across all accounts, counted on every attempt, because each answer says whether an address is free and each emailed invite is mail from the product's own domain to a stranger. `createInvite`: the role must be `admin` or `member` (400 `invalid_role`); the email is normalised and must pass `validEmail` (400 `invalid_email`); the acting member's record must still hold its role (403). The owner's or the inviter's own address is 409 `unavailable`; then, under `lock:team:{owner}`, an existing owner or member login anywhere, or a pending invite for the address in this team, gets the same 409 `unavailable`, and seats used + 1 over the limit gets 409 `{error:"seats", used, limit}`. The token is 32 random bytes (64 hex); only its SHA-256 is stored, as `invite:{hash}` (`{email, role, owner, invitedBy, createdAt, expiresAt, lang}`, TTL 7 days) plus an entry in `invites:{owner}`. The link is `${appOrigin()}/{lang}/app/join#t={token}`: the token is in the fragment, so it never reaches a server or an edge log. When `mailReady()` the link is emailed in the invite's language from a fixed template that names only the inviting login's address (never a display name or brand name, which are free text the sender chose; since 2026-10-08 the mail is multipart and its HTML part, `src/emails/team-invite.ts`, takes the same values and nothing else, rule 688, section 49, with Resend click tracking off because the link carries the token in its fragment), and the link goes to the invited address or nowhere: when that send fails the invite is withdrawn under `lock:team` (`discardInvite`) and the answer is 502 `{error:"send_failed"}` with no link, so an inviter cannot take an address they do not control by making the send fail. Only when mail is not set up at all does the response carry `link` once for the inviter to pass on (the Members page then says a link is created, not that an email is sent). It can never be shown again (only the hash exists); to resend, cancel and invite again. The account's bell gets an item that names neither the invited address nor the role (the bell is shared by every login, and pending invites are admin+) and says whether an email went out. Response: `{ok, invite: {id, email, role, expiresAt}, emailed, link?}`, where `id` is the hash.
4. Cancel (`DELETE /api/team/invite {id}`). Needs `team.invite.cancel` (admin+; an admin cancels `member` invites only; not a founder impersonating). The id must name an invite of this account (else 404); under `lock:team:{owner}` the invite is read again and, if still there, it and its index entry are deleted (404 when an accept holding the lock used it first). Since 2026-10-08 the Members page asks first in the app's confirmation dialog (destructive; focus starts on «رجوع» / "Go back"; rule 695).
5. Join page (`/{lang}/app/join`, public, listed in the app layout's public paths). `JoinForm` reads the token from `location.hash` (`readInviteToken`: 64 hex or nothing), clears the fragment with `history.replaceState`, and never puts the token in a query string. It keeps the token in `sessionStorage` for the tab (`rememberInviteToken`), so switching the page language, which reloads without the fragment, still finds it; it is forgotten after a successful accept and whenever the server calls it invalid, and the page works without storage. The password input is a `PasswordField` with a show toggle (2026-10-08, rule 696). A signed-in visitor is shown who they are signed in as and must log out first; nobody is joined into an existing session. Signed out, the form posts `{token}` to `POST /api/team/accept` for a preview.
6. Preview (`POST /api/team/accept {token}`). Rate limit `team:peek:ip:{ip}` 30 per 15 min. `peekInvite` returns `{team, inviter, email, role, expiresAt}` for a live invite whose owner exists and is not suspended and whose inviter still passes the accept's check (the owner, or an enabled admin of this team inviting a plain member); anything else is 400 `invalid`. Nothing is consumed.
7. Accept (`POST /api/team/accept {token, name, password, lang?}`). Rate limit `team:accept:ip:{ip}` 10 per 15 min. `acceptInvite`: a live invite (400 `invalid`); a name, control characters removed and cut to 80 (400 `invalid_name`); `strongPassword` (400 `weak_password`); bcrypt runs before any lock. Then single use: `inviteuse:{hash}` is taken with SET NX (30 s; a concurrent accept gets 400 `invalid`). Under `lock:team:{owner}` everything that decides the answer is read again, so a cancel, a plan change, a demotion or an account deletion that finished while the accept waited is seen: the invite must still be stored AND in `invites:{owner}` (a cancelled one is 400 `invalid`, never counted as a new seat); the owner exists, is not suspended and is not newer than the invite (an address registered again); an invite sent by a member needs that member to be still an enabled admin of this team and the role to be `member`; and the seats are counted against the owner's limit as it is now, the invite's own seat counted once (409 `seats`). Under `lock:login:{email}` the address must still be free across `user:` and `member:` (409 `unavailable`). The member is written with `acceptedAt = sessionsAfter = now` and the language from the request, else the invite's; the invite and its index entry are deleted. The route then destroys any session on the device, creates one for the member and puts a bell item on the owner. The new login lands in the owner's workspace (section 5).
8. Member login, forgot, reset and password change act on the member record only (sections 2 and 4): the member's own hash and its own `sessionsAfter`; the owner's hash, sessions, MCP token and grants are never touched. A seat-paused member cannot sign in (403 `disabled`).
9. Remove (`DELETE /api/team/member {email}`). Needs `team.remove.member` (admin+); removing an admin needs the owner; a founder impersonating cannot. The member must belong to this account (404). Under `lock:team:{owner}` and then `lock:member:{email}` (the lock every member-record writer holds), the record is read again and, while it still belongs to this account, `member:{email}`, `activebrand:{email}`, the roster entry and every pending invite that member sent are deleted. A profile, password or reset save in flight therefore finishes first or finds nothing: it can never restore a removed login. The member's sessions stop at their next request, because `currentUser` finds no member record (and would refuse one off the roster anyway). Since 2026-10-08 the Members page asks first in the app's confirmation dialog (destructive; focus starts on «رجوع» / "Go back"; rule 695).
10. Leave (`POST /api/team/leave`). Member logins only (`team.leave`; the owner, a token and a founder get 403). The same deletion as a removal, then `destroySession()`. Since 2026-10-08 the Members page asks first in the app's confirmation dialog (destructive; focus starts on «رجوع» / "Go back"; rule 695).
11. Role change (`PATCH /api/team/member {email, role}`). Owner only (`team.role`). `admin` or `member` only; the owner role cannot be assigned, removed or transferred. Written under `lock:team:{owner}` and `lock:member:{email}`; the new role applies at the member's next request. Demoting an admin deletes the invites that admin sent, which could never be accepted afterwards yet would each hold a seat for up to 7 days.
12. Seats. The limit is `seatLimit(owner)` (`src/lib/pricing.ts` since 2026-09-30): `User.seats` when it is a positive number (floored), else `PLAN_SEATS[plan]` (trial 3, starter 1, pro 3, business 7), and Agency Business's 7 unless the founder sets `seats` for its contract; the owner included. A retired `enterprise` record (25 seats) reads as agency, so without a seat override it falls to 7: members above that stay enabled until the next plan or seat change runs `enforceSeats`, while new invites and accepts are refused. Seats used = 1 + members not disabled + unexpired invites. The billing page shows used of the limit to every role.
13. Seats after a plan change. `POST /api/admin/plan` (founder console) runs `enforceSeats(owner)` after any plan or `seats` change, up or down, under `lock:team:{owner}`, relative to who is active now: over the limit, the newest ENABLED members get `disabled: "seats"` (kept, never deleted) and every pending invite beyond the room left is cancelled, the newest first; under it, paused members come back oldest first, but only into seats no pending invite holds. Re-applying the same limit changes nothing (the team is never re-ranked as a whole, so a member the owner brought in after a downgrade is not swapped for one paused earlier). Removing a member does not bring a paused one back by itself; the next limit change does. A disabled member's login answers 403 `disabled` and its sessions stop at their next request. The response and the `admin:log` entry list who was paused and restored and how many invites were cancelled. Trial to Starter therefore pauses up to two members. No other code changes a plan today (`permissions.md` 5.32).
14. Account deletion (`DELETE /api/account`, owner only, or the console's `delete`). `wipeAccount` deletes the account's keys, `user:{owner}` included, and then runs `wipeTeam(owner)` under `lock:team:{owner}`: every member record of this account (each under its own `lock:member`), each member's `activebrand:`, every invite, `members:{owner}` and `invites:{owner}`. An accept that held the team lock first has its member deleted here; one that takes it afterwards finds no owner. A member login never survives to point at an address someone registers again, and a member record older than a re-registered owner is refused anyway (section 5).
15. Owner reset and founder reset both stamp `teamSessionsAfter` on the owner record, in the same locked save as the password, which signs every member out; each signs back in with its own password. A member's own reset or password change stamps only its own `sessionsAfter`. The owner's password change from Settings does not sign members out.
16. Shared and per login. Credits and the plan are the account's, so a member's generations spend the owner's balance under the same per-agent caps. The bell (`notifs:{owner}`) and its read state, the chat ceiling and analytics are per account. The active brand is per login: a member switching or adding a brand moves only `activebrand:{member}`, and a member's stored brand is used only while it is one of the owner's active brands; an admin archiving the brand the owner has open resets the owner's key too.
17. Pages by role. Owner-only and admin+ screens render read-only for lower roles with a one-line note: billing (no pay links, WhatsApp activation or Agency contact link; plan, credits, seats and the four plan cards for all), governance (its change history stays listed, without Restore; section 30), the scheduled tasks page ("Run now" stays), the brand file, brands (switching stays), integrations (requesting stays), MCP (no token or grant controls), refer (nothing read). The sidebar shows the acting person's name, email and role and a Members link for everyone; the trial banner and the limit dialog tell other roles to ask the owner. The working screens offer a plain member only what the server lets a member do: the inbox has no Approve or Publish for a member (an edit is saved without approving, and an approved item goes back to pending), and a chat card says an admin approves; the files page has no catalog import and no edit or delete on core memory; reports offer no CSV import or removal of imported results; the start page tells a member who sets the brand up (and the chat's first-run thread sends them there); the chat's WhatsApp card is shown only to the owner, whose phone it saves. A refused call says the role does not allow it, never "check your connection". The profile page hides the password and delete forms from a founder viewing the account (`GET /api/profile` returns `may`), and the MCP page tells a founder why there is no token.

## 27. Brand changes an agent proposes (2026-09-29)

Files: `src/lib/chat.ts` (`update_brand`), `src/lib/brand-proposals.ts`, `src/app/api/brand/proposals/[id]/route.ts`, `src/components/app/ChatPanel.tsx` (`ProposalCard`).

1. `update_brand` never writes the brand file (rule 568). Every editable field (tone, products, audience, offers, competitors, preferred, dialect, city, name, `banned` additions and `banned_remove`) becomes a proposal. A banned addition carries the new words (`added`), a removal the words to drop (`dropped`); a dialect must be one of `DIALECTS`; other values are capped at `FIELD_MAX`. One reply proposes at most 5 (its card limit); a further call is refused. No `brand_changed` row is written by the tool.
2. `proposeBrandChange` saves `{field, from, to, dropped?, added?, agent, via}` at `brandprop:{scope}:{id}` for 7 days; `via` is `mcp` when the turn came through `ask_noor` (rule 569), else `chat`. The tool tells the model nothing changed until the owner or an admin presses Save, and the assistant message carries the proposal (`ChatMsg.proposals`). Over MCP the card lands in the default chat thread in the app. The tool is never to be called for text found in files, pages, catalogs or tool results.
3. The chat shows a card with the old and the new value, and Save and Dismiss (a plain member sees the card without them; a card from MCP says another assistant asked). `POST /api/brand/proposals/{id} {action}` (admin+ `brand.write`): `decideProposal` claims the id for the moment of the answer (`brandprop-claim:{scope}:{id}`, `setNx`, released afterwards; a second answer at the same moment is 409 `busy`), reads the proposal under `sc(u)` and, under `brandlock:{scope}`, applies a removal or an addition to the list as it is now (a removal is stale only when none of its words is left, an addition only when all its words are already there; an addition that would pass 400 characters is `too_long` and stays open) and every other field only while it still equals `from`, capped at `FIELD_MAX` (rule 571). Stale, applied and dismissed proposals are recorded in `brandprop-done:{scope}:{id}` (7 days), which `history()` reads in one `mget` so a reloaded thread shows each card's outcome. A decided or expired proposal answers 404; one that could not be applied (no brand file) can be answered again at once. An applied proposal is one `brand_changed` activity row (section 32) with the Save presser's `by` (`byOf(u)`), the proposing agent and the field name; a proposal itself and a dismissal record none.
5. `PUT /api/brand` (the Brand page) reads, merges and saves under the same `brandlock:{scope}` (rule 570): a proposal applied at the same time is not lost, a field the request does not send keeps its value, and a lock held past its wait is 409 `busy` with nothing written.
4. Retention: `proposeBrandChange` adds each id to `brandprops:{scope}` before it saves the proposal, and `wipeAccount` deletes, for every brand, every proposal, outcome and claim that set names, the set and `brandlock:{scope}` (`proposalKeys`), so neither the old nor the proposed offers or banned words outlive the account.

## 28. The judge withdraws an auto-approval (2026-09-29)

`judgeTasks` (`src/lib/judge.ts`) writes each verdict under the task lock. When the verdict carries an automatic-fail code and the item is `approved` with no `decidedAt` (auto-approval, no person) and nothing published, the same write sets it back to `pending` (so `saveTask` puts it in `pending:{scope}` and the badge counts it), and one notification per batch tells the owner (`judgeveto:{first task id}`, 7 days). The batch's `judged` and `autofail` activity rows (section 32) are written as before, for the items `updateTask` still found.

## 29. Brands: unlimited, rate-limited, locked (2026-09-30)

Files: `src/app/api/brands/route.ts`, `src/lib/brands.ts`, `src/lib/rate.ts` (`brandAddRefusal`), `src/lib/schedule.ts`, `src/components/app/BrandsPanel.tsx`. Rules 245 and 247, `tests/brand-guardrails.test.ts`.

1. Brands are unlimited on every plan, the trial included; there is no brand cap in code (`BRAND_LIMIT` is gone). `GET /api/brands` answers `{ok, brands, active}` with no `limit`.
2. `POST /api/brands {action:"add", name}` (admin+, `brands.manage`): `brandAddRefusal(u.email)` first, 10 an hour and 30 a day per account in `rate:brandadd:h:{email}` and `rate:brandadd:d:{email}`, shared by the owner and members; the hour is counted first and the day only when the hour allowed it, so an add refused by the hour uses none of the day's 30 (429 `{error: "slow_down", retry: "hour" | "day"}` with `Retry-After` 3600 or 86400, nothing written); then `addBrand` appends to `brands:{email}` under `brandslock:{email}` (`withBrandsLock`: 20 tries at 50 ms, 10 s TTL; 409 `busy` when it cannot be taken, nothing written) and makes the new brand active for the acting login. Adding never answers 402.
3. `rename` and `archive` apply their change to the list as it is under the same lock, so an add that landed in between is kept (409 `busy` on a timeout; 404, 400 `cannot_archive_default` and 400 `bad_action` as before).
4. What an added brand costs: its onboarding, 9 credits (section 6), and whatever its schedule runs. Its Sunday plan starts off (section 16) and its routines are off like every brand's. `BrandsPanel` states the setup cost (from the URL; the manual form is free) and the Sunday-plan default next to the add form (to admins and the owner) and shows the 429 and 409 answers as their own messages: "try again in an hour" or "try again tomorrow" by `retry`, never "shortly". Its Add, Switch, Rename and Archive buttons are off while a change is in flight, so a double click cannot add two brands.

## 30. Governance history and restore (2026-09-29)

Files: `src/lib/actor.ts`, `src/lib/governance.ts` (`saveGovAs`, `govDiff`, `listGovHistory`, `restorePatch`, `rollbackGov`, `historyView`), `src/app/api/governance/route.ts` (PUT), `src/app/api/governance/rollback/route.ts`, `src/app/[lang]/app/governance/page.tsx`, `src/components/app/GovernancePanel.tsx`.

1. Who acted: `byOf(u)` builds a small `By` from the session, never from a request body. It reads the session's actor (section 26): the owner is `{kind: "owner", email}`, a member `{kind: "member", email, role}` with its own login email, a founder impersonating `{kind: "founder", email, imp}` with the owner's email and, in `imp`, the founder's email or `"key"` from the impersonation session (section 14 step 5; never `actor.email`, which is the owner's there), and a connected app `{kind: "token", email}`. No cookie is read: the `nashr_impersonate`, `nashr_return` and `nashr_admin` cookies are values any browser can set, so on their own they never make an owner's action the founder's; only the session record the console writes (`imp`) does. A member role a member cannot hold is dropped, not recorded. A user without an actor never comes from a request (a cron, routine or script user loaded with `getUser`): `byOf` records it as `{kind: "system", job: "cron"}`, never as the owner. Scheduled work passes its own `systemBy(job)`. The whole actor is never stored. Decision records (section 9 step 8), governance revisions and activity rows (section 32) carry it. `imp` stays in storage for the founder console: whatever the account's own logins receive (steps 2 to 4 here, section 32 steps 4 and 5) goes through `publicBy`, which drops it, so support access reads "Thakeen support".
2. Save: `PUT /api/governance` (session first, then owner only: `governance.write`, 403 `forbidden` for an admin or a member, section 26; a founder impersonating may save) reads the body and `byOf(u)` together and calls `saveGovAs(sc(u), body, by)`. Under `govlock:{scope}` (SET NX, 10 s TTL; 40 tries 50 ms apart, else 409 `{error: "busy"}` and nothing changes) it reads the settings, checks and merges the body exactly as `saveGov` always did (`mergeGov`), and compares the two (`govDiff`: dotted paths such as `reviewer`, `autoApprove.social`, `agentCaps.razan`; an auto-approve type that is off equals one never set, a cap `null` equals none). Nothing changed: nothing is written and no revision is kept (the panel sends every field on every Save). Settings that `govRefusal` refuses are not written either and record nothing (400; section 35 step 1): Majed's auto-run on with no cap on an agent he hands work to (`auto_handoff_needs_cap`), or switched from off to on without `confirmAutoHandoff: true` in the body (`auto_handoff_confirm`; the flag is read from the body, never stored). Otherwise the settings and one `GovRevision` `{id (12 hex), at, by, changed, before, after}` are written together, the revision to `govhist:{scope}` (newest first, capped at 100). The answer carries the stored settings and the change as the history lists it (`entry`, or `null`).
3. History: the Governance page reads the settings and `listGovHistory(sc(u))` on the server for every role and passes `historyView` (only the changed settings of each revision, before and after, and `by` without `imp`) to the panel, which lists them newest first: when (UTC), who (Owner, a member's email, Thakeen support, a connected app, Automatic), each changed setting as before → after, a "Restore of an earlier change" badge on restores, and a Restore button for the owner (the read-only page of other roles lists the history without it). Nothing is fetched on mount. Unreadable rows are skipped.
4. Restore: the button asks in the app's confirmation dialog (`ConfirmDialog`, destructive, since 2026-10-08; rule 695), then `POST /api/governance/rollback {id}`: session first (401), owner only like the save (`governance.write`, 403 `forbidden`, before anything is read), 20 per hour per account (`GOV_ROLLBACKS_PER_HOUR`, `rate:govrollback:{email}`, 429 `slow_down`), the id must be 12 hex characters (400 `bad_id`), and it is looked up only in `govhist:{sc(u)}` (404 `not_found` for any other id, another account's included). `restorePatch(rev.before)` builds a full patch from `{...DEFAULT_GOV, ...before}`: every task type on or off and every agent's cap its old number or `null`, because a partial patch keeps the auto-approve types and caps added since; a setting the snapshot lacks takes its default, never the current value. The patch goes through `saveGovAs`, so today's checks apply (a restore that would switch Majed's auto-run back on needs `confirmAutoHandoff: true` in the body, else 400 `auto_handoff_confirm`; the panel then asks with the switch's own words and sends it once more) and it is recorded as a new revision with `rollbackOf`; a restore that changes nothing records nothing. History is never deleted or rewritten. The panel shows the stored settings and prepends the new entry, trimming its list to `CAPS.govHistory` (passed from the page) as the server trims `govhist:`, so it never offers a change the server has dropped. A restore that still gets 404 `not_found` (the change was pushed out, for example by saves in another tab) says the change is no longer in the history and to reload, rather than "try again".
5. Retention: `wipeAccount` deletes `govhist:{s}` and `govlock:{s}` for every brand. Until then a revision keeps the email in its `by` (a member's own login email when a member acted).

## 31. Prompt-version stamps (2026-09-29)

Files: `src/lib/prompt-stamp.ts`, `src/lib/skills.ts` (`playbookHash`, `contentHash`), `src/lib/generate-batch.ts` (`taskFromItem`), `src/lib/golden.ts`, `src/app/api/admin/golden/route.ts`, `src/components/app/GoldenPanel.tsx`.

1. `contentHash(text)` is the first 12 hex characters of SHA-256, or `none` for empty text. `playbookHash(name)` hashes the playbook as this process loaded it, cached next to the text.
2. `promptStamp(type, brand?)`: `skills` (the owning agent's playbook), `review` (review.md, shared by the reviewer and the judge), `industry` (`id:hash` of the industry playbook the brand's batches get, `general` when none matches; left out without a brand) and `code` (`codeHash`: the owning agent's own system prompt, `fixedPrompt` in noor.ts with persona, RULES and OUTPUT FORMAT and a placeholder where its playbook goes; the task spec; the reviewer's checks for the owning agent, `reviewerRubric`; and the judge's rubric, `JUDGE_RUBRIC`; the last two without review.md). Each source has its own hash, so a change made in code is not read as "unchanged".
3. `taskFromItem` sets `Task.pb` on every task of every generated batch (section 8 step 13). MCP `save_draft` drafts have none: no Nashr prompt wrote them.
4. A golden run records `playbooks` (`promptHashes`: every agent playbook and review.md by file name, and `code:{type}` for each case type) at its start (a resumed run keeps its first attempt's), and each result its `pb`. `summarize` sets `promptChanged` per agent by comparing, for each case measured in this run, its `pb` with the same case's `pb` in the previous run (the playbook, review.md, code and, when both have one, industry parts): `null` when no measured case has a stamped one in the previous run or every row of the agent was carried, else whether any part differs. Row by row, never the runs' `playbooks`: a single-agent rerun carries other agents' rows from an older run with their own stamps, and a resumed run keeps the rows its first attempt measured, so a run's rows are not all made with the prompts it started with. That agent's regression lines (not carried rows) end in "(prompt changed)". `GOLDEN_VERSION` still decides which runs compare.

## 32. Per-agent activity log (2026-09-29)

Files: `src/lib/activity.ts`, `src/app/[lang]/app/activity/page.tsx`, `src/components/app/ActivityPanel.tsx`, `src/app/api/activity/route.ts`, `src/app/api/activity/export/route.ts`, and the recorders named below.

1. A row is `{at, kind, agent?, by, via, type?, tasks? (up to 10), count?, credits?, outcome?, score?, autofail?, note?
