The live site is on the old code again about 2 to 3 minutes after the merge (one build). The `live-*` tags make the last good commit obvious. Rolling back a contract release lands on the expand code, which still reads both shapes; going further back than the expand release is what the contract step gave up (item 9).
12. After a rollback. Once a merge is reverted, merging the same commits again adds nothing back. So right after the rollback Claude opens a pull request `production` to `main` that brings the revert into `main`, and the founder merges it. The fix on `main` starts by reverting that revert. The fixed change then comes forward through staging and a normal release pull request (items 3 and 4).

### 52.1 Release checklist: model routing and the Straitly gateway (2026-10-09)

Spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-08-straitly-model-routing-design.md` sections 9 to 11, plan `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-08-straitly-model-routing.md`; rules 810-866 in `tests.md`. Each line is ticked in the release pull request that carries this work, and the release pull request names the step. Keys are set by the founder in the environment or the host's settings, by name only: never in chat, a command line that is saved, a commit or a pull request.

- [ ] Rule 679 step: EXPAND. The new stored fields are optional and additive: `Conversation.model`, `Brand.model` and `User.chinaHostingOk` (section 8 step 15.6). Old code ignores them. No contract release is needed, except a later one that removes the `RATES` fallback from `src/lib/usage.ts` once every metered id is in the catalog.
- [ ] Spike (plan Task 1): the founder sets `STRAITLY_API_KEY` in the shell that runs it, as in the next item. Record the result table in the spec's section 2 for the five unknowns:
  - strict tool schemas (`strict: true`);
  - adaptive thinking with `effort`;
  - the cache counts in `usage` (`cache_read_input_tokens` above 0 on the second identical call);
  - a non-Claude model on `/v1/messages`;
  - the rate limits and the auth header (`x-api-key` or `Authorization: Bearer`).

  The SDK adds `/v1/messages` itself, so the base it is given is `https://api.straitly.ai`, without `/v1`. `STRAITLY_BASE_URL`, when set, is an origin only: the app and the script refuse a `/v1` or any other path, a query, a fragment or user info, and whitespace, a control character or port 0 inside it (rule 810).
- [ ] Catalog ids: in the shell that runs the check, the founder types `read -rs STRAITLY_API_KEY; export STRAITLY_API_KEY` and pastes the key at the silent prompt, so it is neither shown nor saved in the shell history; then run `npx --no-install jiti scripts/check-models.ts`. This reads `GET /v1/models` (rule 812). Exit 0: every catalog id is listed. Exit 1: it prints the ids the list lacks. Exit 2: the check could not be made.
- [ ] Staging first, in this order:
  1. Direct run: while staging.thakeen.com still has `LLM_PROVIDER` unset (direct Anthropic), run the golden set once (next item).
  2. Switch: set `LLM_PROVIDER=straitly` and `STRAITLY_API_KEY` on staging only, keeping `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` for the direct fallback, and rebuild (item 10). Production keeps `LLM_PROVIDER` unset.
  3. Straitly run: run the golden set again.

  To go back to direct Anthropic on staging, unset `LLM_PROVIDER` there and rebuild.
- [ ] Golden set on staging: in the founder console's Golden set panel, press "Run" (`POST /api/admin/golden`; `src/lib/golden.ts`).
  - The two runs of the item above, direct first and Straitly second, at the same `GOLDEN_VERSION` (a constant in `src/lib/golden.ts`: nothing that changes it reaches staging between the runs).
  - Compare the overall score and each agent's judge score. The panel's own note says a move under ±5 is noise.
  - A drop beyond that stops the release.
  - The judge's scores on real work come from `src/lib/evals.ts` (`GET /api/admin/evals?days=N`, the last N days; the console's Quality tab shows 30). Read each agent's average judge score just before the switch and again N days after it, so the two windows do not overlap.
- [ ] Cost and refusals: the founder console's Billing tab, model usage block (rules 855-865):
  - each model's calls and USD;
  - each provider's USD (`anthropic`, `straitly`) and the fallback count (requests re-sent to Anthropic);
  - the refused picks.

  Then check the rates against the first Straitly invoice. This step lowers no Claude rate:
  - The Claude entries in `src/lib/models/catalog.ts` and the `RATES` fallback in `src/lib/usage.ts` stay at the Anthropic LIST price while any route can reach Anthropic directly: the fallback (`withDirectFallback` in `src/lib/model.ts`), and all of production while `LLM_PROVIDER` is unset. Pricing the dearest route keeps the margin floor honest (the comment above `MODELS` in `catalog.ts`).
  - The invoice confirms the gateway's 40% below list (spec section 2), or corrects it. It sets or verifies the gateway-only entries (provider list price x 0.6). It is also the real figure behind the console's caveat: the provider USD there is at catalog (list) rates, so the `straitly` line for a Claude model reads above the invoice.
  - Changing a Claude rate is a separate founder decision, made with `docs/system/costs.md` updated in the same change and a full `npm run check`.
  - Any rate that does change, of either kind, is followed by `npx vitest run tests/pricing-floor.test.ts tests/model-catalog.test.ts` (part of `npm run check` as well).
- [ ] Opus by hand: use an environment with a model key (staging), because without a key chat runs in mock mode and gives the message back.
  - Pick Opus in the chat picker and send one message.
  - The allowance line drops by 2 messages (`chatUnitsFor`, rule 818).
  - The overflow hold is 4 credits (`chatHoldFor`, rule 817). It shows as `chat.overflow.hold` in `GET /api/chat?conv={id}` for that conversation (and in the `chat` field of the reply). On screen it shows only with the allowance used up: in the line under the box when "continue on credits" is on, or in the `chat_credits` limit dialog.
- [ ] Before any economy model becomes pickable (`pick: true` on an entry whose chat multiplier is below 1), both of these must be done:
  - Chat says that a message "counts as 1 message". Today the line appears only when `chatUnits` is above 1 (rule 846).
  - `POST /api/conversations` answers the new conversation's `chat` status. Today the allowance line keeps the previous conversation's ceiling until the first answer (rule 853, "Not pinned").
- [ ] Production: `LLM_PROVIDER=straitly` reaches production only after the founder's explicit yes in chat for that change, and in a release pull request that names it (items 3 and 10). Without that yes, production stays on direct Anthropic.

## 53. Email verification: signup, login, existing accounts, change email (2026-10-08)

Spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-08-email-verification-design.md` (the code is the truth where it differs: the bucket names in item 5 and the session window in item 9). Files: `src/lib/auth.ts` (`emailVerified`, `currentUserUnverified`, the gate in `resolveSession`), `src/lib/verify-email.ts` (code, limits, `markVerified`, `moveUnverifiedAccount`), `src/app/api/auth/verify/send|check|email/route.ts`, `src/app/api/auth/signup/route.ts`, `src/app/[lang]/app/verify/page.tsx`, `src/components/app/VerifyForm.tsx`, `src/lib/verify-client.ts`, `src/app/[lang]/app/layout.tsx`, `src/lib/mcp.ts`, `src/lib/schedule.ts`, `src/lib/secretary-remind.ts`, `src/lib/secretary-feed.ts`, `src/app/api/auth/login/route.ts`, `src/app/api/auth/reset/route.ts`, `src/components/app/AuthForm.tsx`. Rules 751-769, `tests/email-verify-*.test.ts`. A follow-up spec, not this one, adds WhatsApp phone verification.

1. Who is gated. Every owner login (`user:{email}`) whose record has no `emailVerifiedAt`. A member login is gated by its owner's state (rule 767): while the owner has not verified, the account spends nothing, so its members are signed out too, in both resolvers, and come back on their own once the owner verifies. A founder impersonation passes (support must reach a stuck account), the console key's `imp: "key"` included. Existing accounts are not grandfathered: on release day none has the stamp, so each owner, the founder included, meets the gate on the next request. `createUser` sets `gatedSignup: true` from release day on; it marks an account created under the gate and is never cleared.
2. Signup. After the account and its session exist, the route mails the first code and, only when that mail went out, counts it in `verify:gap:{email}`, `verify:hour:{email}` and `verify:day:{email}` (a failed mail is not counted, so the verify page's own first send goes through), and answers `{ok, referred, partner, verify: true, mail: boolean}`. A mail that cannot go out (mail off, the provider refused, the call threw) never fails the signup; `mail: false` sends the form to `/{lang}/app/verify?next=...&sent=0`, and the page then offers a resend instead of saying a mail went. The form always goes to the verify page with the old destination (the start page, or `next`) in `next`.
3. Login. The password still creates an owner's session. The answer is `{ok: true, verify: boolean}`: `verify: true` for an owner who has not verified, and `LoginForm` then goes to `/{lang}/app/verify?next=` with the `safeNext`'d destination (or `/{lang}/app`) instead of the destination, so a `next` outside `/[lang]/app` cannot loop back to login. The OAuth consent page (`/oauth/authorize`) sends a signed-out visitor to `/ar/app/login?next=/oauth/authorize?...`; `safeNext` keeps that path, so after the code the verify page returns to the consent page. An owner who opens any app page with an old session lands on the verify page through the layout (item 4). A member of an unverified owner gets 403 `owner_unverified` and no session (the form says «صاحب الحساب لم يؤكد بريده بعد»); a member's password reset saves the password and answers the same 403. An owner's password reset verifies the address (rule 769): the single-use link was mailed to that address, so `/api/auth/reset` stamps `emailVerifiedAt` when unset, inside the lock that saves the new hash.
4. The gate. `currentUser()` returns `null` for an unverified owner, so every page and route that calls it (also the ones added later) treats the owner as signed out. `currentUserUnverified()` resolves the same session and also returns that owner; only the app layout, the verify page and the three verify routes call it (rule 752, a source scan). The app layout, when `currentUser()` is null and the path is not public, asks `currentUserUnverified()` and, for an owner, redirects to `/{lang}/app/verify`, keeping the requested page in a `safeNext`'d `?next=`; otherwise to login. `/verify` is in the layout's public list. The verify page sends a signed-out visitor to login and a member, a founder session or a verified owner to `?next=` or the app. A member of an unverified owner resolves in neither resolver, so the layout and the verify page send them to login. The MCP bearer (`userFromBearer`) refuses an unverified owner (rule 753), tokens minted before the gate included.
5. The code. `sendVerifyCode` issues 6 digits from `crypto.randomInt` (`issueVerifyCode`), stores `{hash, sentAt}` under `verify:code:{email}` with a 900 s TTL (SHA-256 salted with the address, never the code: that keeps the code out of storage and logs, but 10^6 codes reverse offline for anyone who can read Redis, hence the 15-minute TTL and the attempt cap), and mails it in the account's language (plain text through `sendEmail`). A new code replaces the old one and deletes `verify:tries:{email}`. Send is `POST /api/auth/verify/send`, for the session's address only. Refusals, in order, the refusing one names the wait with `Retry-After`: 503 `mail_unavailable` when `mailReady()` is false (unless a test deploy, item 10); 429 `slow_down` from `verify:gap:{email}` (1 per 60 s), `verify:hour:{email}` (5 per hour), `verify:day:{email}` (10 per day, rule 768, `Retry-After: 86400`) and `verify:ip:{ip}` (20 per hour). 401 without a session (and for a member of an unverified owner); 409 `already_verified` for a member of a verified owner or a verified owner; 502 `send_failed` when the provider refuses the mail, and the code is dropped then (a code nobody received must not be left to guess against). The page shows a 60 s resend countdown, taken from the answer's `resendIn` or the `Retry-After`.
6. Check. `POST /api/auth/verify/check {code}`: 401 without a session or for a member of an unverified owner, ok for a member of a verified owner or a verified owner (a double submit lands here), 429 `slow_down` past 60 per IP per hour (`verifycheck:ip:{ip}`). Arabic-Indic and Persian digits are turned into Latin; input that is not exactly 6 digits then is 400 `wrong_code` and uses no attempt. Otherwise `checkVerifyCode` reads the code (`expired` when gone), takes an attempt with `INCRBY verify:tries:{email}` (TTL 900 s, same as the code) before comparing with `sameSecret`, so parallel guesses each get their own count: a wrong answer is 400 `wrong_code` with `attemptsLeft`, the fifth deletes the code and the counter (`locked`), and a request that finds the counter already past five also answers `locked`. A correct code calls `markVerified`: under the account lock it sets `emailVerifiedAt` only if unset, then deletes the code and the counter, and the page goes to `next` or `/{lang}/app` (onboarding for a new account, as before). A parallel submit that lost the race and finds the account verified answers ok.
7. The verify page. `/{lang}/app/verify`, Arabic first, quiet-D (rule 519). One numeric input (`inputmode="numeric"`, `autocomplete="one-time-code"`, left to right, wide enough for Arabic digits), a resend button disabled while the countdown runs, the attempts left after a wrong code (Arabic special-cases one and two: «باقي محاولة واحدة», «باقي محاولتان»), and Log out. The address shown is the session's, never one from the URL. It says "We sent a 6-digit code to {email}" only while a mail is known to have gone; after a signup with `sent=0`, a refused send or a change-email answer with `mail: false` it says «بريدك: {email}» / "Your email: {email}" and offers a resend at once. A first visit with no code waiting sends one when the page opens (`autoSend`, once). When this deploy cannot send mail (`mailReady()` false, and not a test deploy) the page shows the support link at once: WhatsApp (`SUPPORT_WHATSAPP`), or a neutral "Contact us" link to the public help page when no number is set. The code is never in a URL or a log.
8. Change a mistyped address. "Wrong email? Change it" shows only for an owner with `gatedSignup` and no `emailVerifiedAt`; `POST /api/auth/verify/email {email}` enforces it (403 `not_allowed` for any other account, a member session or a founder impersonating; 401 without a session or for a member of an unverified owner). Order: `verifymove:ip:{ip}` 20 per hour (429), `bad_email` and `same_email` (400), `verifymove:acct:{session email}` 10 per day, counted before the lookup so a taken address is no oracle (429), then `moveUnverifiedAccount`: the login locks of both addresses in alphabetical order, then the old account's user lock; 3 moves per account per UTC day (429 with `Retry-After` to the next UTC midnight; the counter is `emailMoves` and travels with the record); the target must be free in `getLogin` (owner or member), else 409 `exists`. Inside: write the record at the new address (name, phone, password hash, lang, plan, credits, trial end, `createdAt`, `gatedSignup`), register its referral code, carry the referral applied at signup and the partner attribution, drop the old address's pending code, and only then `wipeAccount(old)`. An account that is still gated and unverified holds no brand data (the gate blocked every route), which is why this small move is enough; an older account (no `gatedSignup`) never sees the option, because its data is keyed by its address. A move that stopped before the wipe leaves two records; the retry recognises its own copy (same `createdAt`, same password hash, still gated and unverified) and finishes instead of answering `exists`. On success the route ends the old session, opens one for the new address and sends a code to it through the same refusals as item 5; it answers 200 `{ok, email, mail?}` even when that send is refused or fails (the account has moved; `mail: false` and the page offers a resend), 429 `slow_down` when a bucket is full.
9. What a move leaves behind. The moved record gets a fresh `sessionsAfter` (the spec listed it as carried; it is not): a session issued earlier for the target address (a deleted former owner, a removed member, this owner's own devices after A to B to A) must never resolve to the moved account, and the session the route opens right after is not older than it. `wipeAccount(old)` does what any account deletion does, so it appends an entry to the `deleted-accounts` list and drops the old address's `uev:` signup counters: the founder's funnel counts the signup of a moved account under neither address (it undercounts). The referrer's list and the partner's set may name both addresses after a stopped move, until the retry.
10. `devCode`. Only when `APP_ENV=test` and the deploy is not a production deploy (`devCodeExposed`, rule 766) the send, signup and change-email answers carry the code as `devCode`, and they do so even when mail is off or refused, so the e2e suite verifies an account without a mailbox (`signUpVerified` in `e2e/fixtures/accounts.ts`). No other answer, log or URL ever carries a code. Hostinger and Vercel production must never set `APP_ENV=test` (`variables.md`).
11. What pauses until the owner verifies (rules 755 and 756). `dailyRoster()` puts an unverified owner's scopes in `idle`: the `daily`, `evening`, `weekly`, `routines` and `stores` crons skip them (no brief, lifecycle email, scheduled task or credit spend), and so does everything in `dailyFor` for them: renewal, top-up settlement and hold refunds wait too, and only Reem's purge of items deleted over 30 days ago still runs for the idle scopes. The reminder sweep (`cron/secretary`) leaves their reminders in `sec:due` and delivers them late, once the owner is verified. `cron/media` keeps finishing and refunding jobs already started and paid (a paid result is not thrown away) and `cron/golden` is the founder's evaluation; neither changed. Calendar feed and booking links answer as for an unknown owner, a member's link too while the owner behind it is unverified. Consequence the founder accepted: an existing customer's automations and booking page pause until they verify, then resume on their own.
12. The founder console. The founder's cookie access goes through `currentUser`, so after release the founder must verify their own email first; `WAITLIST_ADMIN_KEY` access (header or key session) does not depend on a login and still works. Impersonating an unverified owner works. Since 2026-10-09 (rules 776-777) the Users list marks an owner without `emailVerifiedAt` "Unverified" («غير موثّق»), the account panel shows the day the address was proved, and an unverified owner's panel offers "Resend verification code": `POST /api/admin/users/{email} {action: "resendVerification"}` mails a fresh code to the account's own address through the same `verifySendRefusal` as item 5 (the per-address buckets are shared with the owner's own resend), never returns the code (not even on a test deploy), answers 409 `already_verified` for a verified owner and 502 `send_failed` when the mail did not go out (the code is then dropped), and logs `resendVerification` with `sent` to `admin:log`. The founder never sees or types the code; the owner still enters it on the verify page.
13. Release. Resend must stay configured (`RESEND_API_KEY`, `MAIL_FROM`) on Hostinger: without it nobody can verify and the page shows only the support link. Run the staging smoke before the release pull request (section 52).

## 54. Agent scope: four layers (2026-10-10)

Files: `src/lib/agent-scope.ts`, `src/lib/agent-scope-classify.ts`, `src/lib/agent-scope-verdicts.ts`, `src/lib/notes-read.ts`, `src/lib/scope-refusal.ts`, `src/lib/rate.ts`, `src/lib/chat.ts`, `src/lib/generate-batch.ts`, `src/app/api/chat/route.ts`, `src/app/api/generate/route.ts`, `src/app/api/mcp/route.ts`, `src/components/app/GeneratePanel.tsx`, `src/components/app/ReportsPanel.tsx`. Spec: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-08-entitlements-agent-scope-design.md` section 6. Branch `claude/entitlements-part-b`; the other layers are later tasks on it.

The founder sets each layer's mode in `agent-scope:modes` (`prompt` on or off; `input`, `role`, `output` off, shadow or enforce; default on, shadow, shadow, shadow, rule 790). A layer in `shadow` asks the classifier and logs what it would have refused without changing the answer; in `enforce` a refusal replaces the answer. The classifier (`classify`, Haiku through `route("agentScope")`, 3 s, rule 791) answers with one of six verdicts; every verdict but `in` is logged to `agent-scope:log:{UTC day}` (rule 793).

1. Chat turn: input gate (rules 794, 795). The web chat (`POST /api/chat`) and MCP `ask_noor` read the modes and the brand scope's ceiling of refused messages with the burst ceiling, in one round (`agentScopePreflight(u)`). In `enforce`, a scope that has had 20 refused messages in the hour or 60 in the day (`rate:agent-scope:h|d:{scope}`, the brand scope `sc(u)`) is answered first: 429 `slow_down` with `retry` and `Retry-After` (MCP: the slow text with `isError`), before the allowance is touched and with no classifier call. Otherwise `chat()` gets the modes and, after the paused reply and the brand reads and before the first model call, builds the context: the agent, `where` (`chat` or `mcp_chat`), the active brand's name and sector, the owner's other brands (`otherBrandNames`, `[]` when the store fails) and the request's deadline. `off`: nothing. `shadow`: the classifier runs alongside the turn, is awaited before every return, and a refusal is only logged; the turn is counted as usual. `enforce`: the classifier is asked first; `in` runs the turn; any other verdict saves the owner's message and the refusal (written from the owner's own words, rule 792) to the thread and returns it with no model call and no `usd`, so `runChatTurn` gives the message back to the allowance, and the refusal is counted toward the ceiling (only enforced input refusals count). A classifier error or a timeout lets the turn through (input fails open), and so does a ceiling that cannot be read.
2. Generation notes: input gate (rule 796). `POST /api/generate` and MCP `generate_content` hand the owner's notes to `generateBatch` as `notesGate`, and the gate runs inside it, so a refusal charges nothing and saves nothing and an owner who is refused for a real reason reads that reason. Order: auth; the model-paused 503 (web); the type check (400 `bad_type`; MCP "Unknown type."); `agentScopePreflight`; then `generateBatch(…, { agentScope: modes, notesGate })`, which checks the model switch (paused), the brand (`no_brand`), the trial (`trial_over`) and the missing data (`missing_data`), and only then the gate, before the renewal, the owning agent's cap and the charge. A trial that has ended, a missing brand file or a missing cart store therefore make no classifier call, count nothing and read their own answer in every mode. Notes are a string: the web route drops any other value, so an array that the generator would read as its text never passes unread. The gate asks, counts and refuses nothing when `input` is `off` or the notes are empty after trimming, so a spent refusal ceiling (below) never holds a request without notes. Otherwise: (a) the preflight's spent ceiling (item 1: 20 refused requests in the hour or 60 in the day, only in `enforce`) is 429 `slow_down` with `retry`, `Retry-After` 3600 or 86400 and the slow text as `message` (MCP: the slow text with `isError`), with no classifier call; (b) the ask is counted against the scope's bound on classifier calls for generation, 60 an hour and 200 a day (`rate:agent-scope-gen:h|d:{scope}`, `agentScopeGenRefusal`), which the free refusals cannot reach but `no_credits` and `agent_cap` still can: over the bound, `enforce` answers the same 429 `slow_down` (the window that is spent, the day when both are) and `shadow` skips the classifier, logs nothing and lets the batch go on to its own answer; (c) the classifier reads the notes trimmed and cut at the first 2000 characters, the ones the generator and the reviewer read (`GENERATE_NOTES_READ`, one constant), with the owning agent of the type (`agentForTask`: Dana for a report, Razan for finance and so on), the brand's name and sector, the names of the other brands (`[]` when that read fails, printing only the error's name) and the request's deadline; `where` is `generate` or `mcp_generate`. The reply language is the request's `x-lang` else the account's on the web, the account's over MCP. `shadow`: a refusal is logged; the check runs beside the batch, which is charged as usual, and `generateBatch` awaits it before it returns on every path (a saved batch, a refusal at the charge, a failure). `enforce`: the check is awaited before the charge; a verdict other than `in` is 422 `{ok:false, error:"agent_scope", message}` on the web or a tool error with the same text over MCP, and counts toward the ceiling of item 1; credits, tasks and the activity log are untouched. A classifier error or timeout, or a bound counter that cannot be read, lets the batch run (input fails open); a paused or mocked model asks nothing. The Generate page and the Reports page (the report buttons and the cart-recovery draft) show `message` of an `agent_scope` or `slow_down` answer as text, through `scopeRefusalText` (`src/lib/scope-refusal.ts`). The classifier's cost goes to the global meter only, never to the owner.
